C:UAPTER 16. No.4,
MOTOR VEHICLES INSURANCE (THIRD-PARTY
RISKS).
Regulations made under sections 7 and 24 of the Motor Vehicles Insurance
(Third-party Risks) Ordinance.
PART 1.
1. Thes.e regulations may be cited as The Motor Vehicles Insurance (Third-party Risks) Regulations.
2. In these regulations the following expressions have the meanings hereby respectively assigned to them:-
"the Ordinance" means the Motor Vehicles Insurance (Third-party Risks)
Ordinance;
" policy" means such policy of insurance in respect of third party risks
arising out of the use of motor vehicles as complies with the requirements of the Ordinance and includes a single covering note issued for a period not exceeding ninety days in respect of any motor vehicle, but does not include any extension of a covering note or any subsequent covering note issued in respect of the same motor vehicle during any period of six consecutive months;
" security" means such security in respect of third party risks arising out of
the use of motor vehicles as complies with the requirements of the Ordinance;
" company" means an insurer as defined in section 2 of the Ordinance;
" owner" in relation to a: vehicle which is the subject of a hiring agreement
or hire purchase agreement means the person in possession of the vehicle under that agreement.
3. (i) A company shall issue to every holder of a security or of a policy other than a covering note issued by the company:---:-
(a) in the case of a policy or security relating to a specified vehicle or to
specified vehicles a certificate of insurance in form A or a certificate of a security in form D set out in the Schedule to these regulations in respect of each such vehicle.
(b) in the case of a policy or security not relating to any specified vehicle or
vehicles such number of certificates in the form B or D set out in the Schedule to these regulations as may be necessary to enable the requirements of subsec-tion (1) of section 18 of the Ordinance and of these regulations as to the produc-tion of evidence that a motor vehicle is not being driven in contravention of section 3 of the Ordinance to be complied with:
Provided that where a security is intended to cover the use of more than
10 motor vehicles at one time the company by whom it was issued may subject to the consent of the Governor issue one certificate only and where such consent has been given the holder of the security may issue duplicate copies of such certificate duly authenticated by him up to such number and subject to such conditions as the Governor may determine.
(ii) Every policy in the form of a covering note issued by a company shall have printed thereon or on the back thereof a certificate of insurance in the form C set out in the Schedule to these regulations.
T.-VIII. 39 4. (i) Every certificate of insurance or certificate of security shall be duly authen-ticated by or on behalf of the company by whom it is issued.
(ii) The certificate aforesaid shall be issued on or before the date on which the policy cir security is issued or renewed. .
5. Where under the terms of a policy or security relating to a specified motor vehicle the holder is entitled to drive any other motor vehicle than that specified without contravention of section 3 of the Ordinance, the company by which the policy or security was issued may and shall on demand being made to them by the holder issue to him a further certificate of insurance in form A or B set out in the Schedule to these regulations or a further cerfificate of security as the case may be.
6. The following evidence that a motor vehicle is not being driven in contravention of section 3 of the Ordinance may be prodllced by the. driver of such motor vehicle on the request of a member of the Police Force in pursuance of section 18 of the Ordinance as an alternative to the production of a certificate of insurance or a certi-ficate of security;-
(a) a duplicate copy of a certificate of security issued in accordance with
regulation 3 (i) (b);
(b) in the case of the municipal motor vehicles mentioned in subsection (4) of
section 3 of the Ordinance, a certificate in the form E set out in the Schedule to these regulations signed by some person authorised in that behalf by such authority.
7. Any certificate issued in accordance with sub-paragraph (b) of the preceding regulation shall be destroyed by the person by whom it was issued before the motor vehicle to which it relates is sold or otherwise disposed of.
8. (aJ Every certificate issued in pursuance of the Ordinance and of these regula-tions shall be printed and completed in black on white paper or similar material.
(b) No certificate so issued shall contain any advertising matter either on the face or on the back thereof:
Provided that the name and address 6f a company by which a certificate is issued' or a reproduction of the seal of the company or any monogram or similar device of the company or the name and address of an insurance broker shall not be deemed to be advertising matter for the purposes of this regulation if it is printed or stamped at the foot or on the back of such certificate. '
9. Any person applying under section 13 of the Motor Vehicles and Road Traffic Ordinance for the licensing of a motor vehicle shall produce to the Licensing Officer, for verification, a c!,:rtificate of insurance or a certificate of security issued in accord-ance with regulation 3 (i) Ca) of these regulations, indicating that on tJ?e date when the licence comes into operation there will be in force a policy or a security in relation to the use of the motor vehicle by the applicant or by other persons on his' order or with his permission;
Provided that there maybe produced in lieu thereof:-
(i) in the case of a motor vehicle of which the owner had deposited with the
Accountant General the sum of $48,000.00 in accordance with the provisions of subsection (3) of section 5 of the Ordinance, a certificate signed by the owner of the vehicle or by some person authorised by him in that behalf and by the Accountant General that such deposit has been made;
(ii) in the case where the motor vehicle is one of more than 10 motor vehicles
owned by the same person in respect of which a policy or p0licies of insurance have been obtained by him from the same insurer, a statement duly authenti-cated by the insurer to the effect that on the date when the licence becomes operative an insurance policy which complies With the Ordinance will bein force in relation to the motor vehicle; ,
(iii) in the case of motor vehicles used and employed exclusively in Govern-
ment service or in the service of the Municipal Authorities of Port-of-Spain, San Fernando or Arima, a certificate signed by some person authorised in that behalf that the vehicles in respect of which the application for a licence is made are owned by Government or by either of the said authorities and so used and employed exclusively.
10. (a) Every company by whom a policy or a security is issued shall keep a ;record of the following particulars relative thereto and of any certificates issued in connection therewith:-
(i) Full name and address of the person to whom the policy, security or
certificate is issued.
(ii) In the case of a policy relating to a specified motor vehicle or to specified
motor vehicles the index mark and registration number of each such motor vehicle.
(iii) The date on which the policy or security comes into force and the date
on ,vhich it expires.
(iv) In the case of a policy the conditions subject to which the persons or
classes of persons specified in the policy will be indemnified.
(v) In the case of a security the conditions subject to which the undertaking
,given by the company under the security will be implemented.
(vi) Every such record shall be preserved for one year from the date of
expiry of the policy or security.
(b) Every Municipal Authority mentioned in subsection (4) of section 3 of the Ordinance shall keep a record of the motor vehicles owned by them in respect of which a policy or a security has not been obtained, and of any certificate issued by them under these regulations in respect of such motor vehicles, and of the with- ,drawal or destruction of any such certificates.
(e) Any person, authority, or company by whom records of documents are :required to be kept by these regulations shall, without charge, furnish to the Com-missioner of Police on request any particulars thereof.
(d) Every company by whom a certificate of insurance or a certificate of security is issued, shall forthwith forward to the Licensing Officer, Port-of-Spain, a .duplicate of such certificate.
For the purposes of this paragraph, the expression" Licensing Officer" means an ,officer appointed under section 4 of the Motor Vehicles and Road Traffic Ordinance.
11. vVnere to the knowledge of a company a policy or security issued by them ,ceases to be effective without the consent of the person to whom it was issued other-wise than by effluxion of time or by reason of his death the company shall forthwith notify the Commissioner of Police of the date on which the policy or security ceased ·;to be effective.
12. \A,I'bere with the consent of the person to whom it was issued a policy or security is suspended or ceases to be effective otherwise than by effluxion of time such person shall forthwith return any relative certificates to the company by which they were issued and a new policy or security shall not be issued to that person, nor shall the said policy or security be transferred to any other person unless and until the certifi- ,cates have been returned to the company or the company are satisfied that they have been lost or destroyed.
13. (1) Where any company by whom a certificate of insurance or a certificate of :security has been issued are satisfied that the certificate has become defaced or has been lost or destroyed they shall if requested so to do by the person to whom the -certificate was issued, issue to him a fresh certificate.
(2) Every statutory declaration made for the purposes of paragraph (e) of ,subsection (2) of section 8 or of section 12 of the Ordinance, shall be delivered forth-with to the insurer by the person making such declaration.
PART II.
14. In this Part of these regulations the following expressions have the meanings :hereby respectively assigned to them:-
"issuing authority" means the Trinidad Automobile Association;
" visitor" means a person bringing a motor vehicle into the Colony and making
only a temporary stay therein;
" motor vehicle" means a motor vehicle brought into the Colony by a; visitor
39 (2)
15. A visitor who is a holder of a policy of insurance issued outside the Colony in
respect of third party risks arising out of the driving by him of a motor vehicle in the Colony may make application to the issuing authority for a certificate (hereinafter called" a certificate of foreign insurance ") in the form F set out in the Schedule to these regulations.
16. The issuing authority may issue in duplicate a certificate of foreign insurance to any visitor who makes application therefor ip. t~~ .. mer prescribed by these regulations. ' ' ' i~' • '
17. Every such' application as aforesaid shall be signed by the person by whom it is made and shall specify the number of the policy in respect of third party risks held by him, the name of the company by which it was issued, the date on which the policy commences and the date on which it expires, and shall also contain a declara-tion by the applicant that the provisions of the policy with respect to third party risks are effective in relation to the driving of the motor vehicle in the Colony by him or by some other person or persons or classes of persons specified in the declara-tion.
18. Every certificate of foreign insurance shall be signed by some person duly authorised in that behalf by the issuing authority.
19. The period of validity of a certificate of foreign insurance shall not exceed either of the following:-
(a) four months from the date of the arrival of the motor vehicle in the
Colony;
(b) the unexpired period covered by the policy to which it relates.
20. For the purposes of the Ordinance and of regulation 6 of Part I of these regUlations, a certificate of foreign insurance shall have effect as if it were a certificate of insurance issued by an insurer and the policy of insurance to which it relates shall ,be deemed to comply with the requirements of the Ordinance.
21. The provisions of section 6 of the Ordinance shall not apply in relation to any policy of insurance in respect of which a certificate of foreign insurance has been issued. '
22. A. certificate of foreign insurance shall be forthwith returned by the visito.r to the issuing authority iithe motor vehicle to which it relates is sold or otherwise disposed of or if by reason of his obtaining a new policy or otherwise a new certificate of foreign insurance is issued to him during his stay in the Colony, and if the certifi-cate is not so returned it shall be surrendered to the issuing authority by which.it was, issued by or on behalf of the visitor when :the motm vehicle is taken out "of the·; Ccl=y . ' . ','
23. The issuing authority shall keep a record of thefoll6wing particulars relative, to any certificates of foreign insurance issued by it :-, ,
, .. (i) The fllllname and address of the person to whom the certificate is 5.ss11ed and particulars of the persons or classes of persons authorised to 'drive the motor vehicle;
(ii) the date on which the policy of insurance to which the certificate relates.
commences and the date on which it expires;' , ' .
(iii) the date of return of the certificate to theissuing authority; and the issuing authority shall without charge furnish to the CommissionE:)r' of Police on request any particulars thereof.
PART III.
24. Any person acting in contravention of or failing to comply with these regula-tions shall for each offence be liable on summary co~viction by a Magistrate to a fine of twenty-four dollars. '

CHAPTER 16. No.5.
LICENSING OF VEHICLES.
Regulations lllade under section 36 of the Licensing, of Vehicles Ordinance.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Licensing of Vehicles Regulations.
2. For the purposes of the above-named Ordinance the forms set out in the First Schedule hereto, or forms to the like effect, may be used in the cases to which they refer, with such variation as a Licensing Authority or the Inspection Officer, as the case may be, may consider desirable.
LICENCE PLATES.
3. The licence plate shall, in addition to the number and year of the licence issued in respect of the vehicle, bear an index mark indicating the class of vehicle licensed.
4. The index mark shall be the letter or letters shown opposite to the appropriate class _ of vehicle given below:-
Class of Vehicle. Inde.'V mark.
Agricultural Cart A.C.
Bicycle ... B.
Donkey Cart D.C.
Hand Cart H.C.
Other Carriage ... C.
Other Cart O.C.
Private Carriage P.C.
Tram Car T.e.
Tricycle T.
5. The Jicence plate shall be fixed in an upright position so that every letter or figure on the plate is upright and easily distinguishable, and shall ordinarily be placed in the positions folloWing:-
Ja) On bicy~les and tricycles, affixed in any conspicuous place at the rear of
same;
(b) On carriages, affixed by screws on the outside right of the driver's seat;
(c) On carts, affixed by screws to the outside of the right or off shaft;
(d) On tram cars, affixed by screws in any conspicuous place in front or at
the rear of same.
PUBLIC CONVEYANCES.
6. The owner of every hackney carriage shall, in addition to affixing a licence plate, keep distinctly painted on such carriage;-
(a) the number of the certificate of fitness granted by the Inspection Officer
in respect of such carriage, prefixed by the district letter assigned under the next succeeding Regulation to the District in which the licence was granted. Such number and letter shall be painted in the centre of the panel at the back on a black ground in white figures 2t inches in height, and every part of such figures shall be not less than % of an inch thi2k.
(b) the number of persons which, under the terms of the licence, may be
carried in such carriage. Such number shall be painted on the outside right of the driver's seat on a black ground in conspicuous :figures.
7. The District letter indicating the District of the Licensing Authority by whom a licence was issued shall be the letter shown opposite to the name of that district given below:-
District. District Letter.
Port-of-Spain A
San Fernando B
Arima C
Blanchisseuse D
Cedros ... E
Couva F
Chaguanas G
Manzanilla H
Mayaro I
Montserrat K
Naparima L
Oropuche and La Brea M
St. Ann's o
Diego Martin P
Savana Grande and Moruga R
Tacarigua S
Tobago ... T
Taco X
8. Every Inspection Officer shall keep Registers in the prescribed form of all certi-ficates .of fitness granted by him in respect of hackney carriages.
9. The o\vner of every cart shall, in addition to affixing a licence plate, cause his full name and address and the distinguishing number of his licence to be painted upon some conspicuous part of the right or off-side of such cart in legible letters and figures not less than one inch in height and in a different colour from tht; ground on which such letters and :figures are painted.
10. The owner of a vehicle who contravenes any of the above regulations and the owner and driver of any vehicle in respect of which any of the requirements of these regulations have .not been complied with shall for every offence be liable to a'penalty of $48.00.

X RIVET HOL.ES.
CHAPTER 16. No.6.
HACKNEY CARRIAGES.
Regulations m.ade under section 32 of the Hackney Carriages ~fnance.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Hackney Carriages Regulations. 2. In these Regulations the expression "the Ordinance" means the Hackney Carriages Ordinance.
3. The forms set out in Schedule I hereto, or forms to the like effect, may be used in the cases to which they refer, with such variations as the Commissioner of Police may make from time to time.
4. Drivers of hackney carriages shall be entitled to charge the fares set out in Schedule II hereto. .
5. Every owner of a hackney carriage shall cause a statement of the fares prescribed for his district to be printed or painted on a suitable plate in legible letters and figures of such a colour as to be clearly distinguishable from the colour of the ground whereon such letters and figures are printed or painted. He shall cause such plate to be fixed on the inside of the splashboard Of such carriage or other-wise in such a position and manner that the letters and figures on such plate shall be at all times distinctly and plainly visible and legible to any person being conveyed in such carriage. . .
Such owner shall, from time to time, as often as occasion may require,renewsuch plate or the letters and figures of such plate, and he shall not, nor shall any driver of such carriage, wilfully or negligently cause or allow such plate to be inverted or detached or the letters or figures on such plate to be in any manner or by any means concealed from the view of any person being conveyed in such carriage at any time while such carriage may ply or be used for hire.
6. Every driver ~f a hackney carriage shall, at all times, when standing,plying, or driving for hire, conduct himself in an orderly manner, anG[ with civility and propriety towards every person seeking to hire, or hiring, or being conveyed .in.such,· carriage; and shall comply with every reasonable requirement of any person hiring or being conveyed in such carriage.
7. Every owner of a hackney carriage shall cause the seats of such carriage to .be properly cushioned or covered, and the floor of such carriage to be provided with p. proper carpet, mat or other suitable covering. He shall cause such carriage to be so furnished and fitted .in all other respects as to secure cleanliness, and shall make dlle provision for the safety and convenience of every person conveyed in such carriage.
8. The driver of a hackney carriage shall be entitled to charge 4 cents for each package over 20 lb. weight carried as luggage. .
9. Hackney Carriages standing for hire in stands shall leave such stands .for the purpose of answering calls according to their priority of position in such stands and not otherwise.

T.-VIII. 40
CHAPTER 17.· No. 1.
RAILWAYS.
Tolls, Rules and Regulations under sections 15 and 16 of the Railways
Ordinance.
1. These tolls, rules and regulations may be cited as the Railway Tolls, Rules and Regnla tions.
2. The Railway will not be responsible for any error, misprint, or omission in the Tariff.
3. Complaints of incivility or want of attention orilflie part of the Raihvay Staff should be addressed to the General Manager of Railways, Railway Station, Port-of-Spain.
·4. The times of the trains given in the time bills are those before which the trains will not start and by which intended passengers must be at the stations with their luggage labelled, and tickets taken ready to proceed, but the Department does not undertake that the trains shall start or arrive at the time specified on the bills, nor will it be accountable for any loss, inconvenience or injury which may arise from delays or detentions occasioned by incidence of traffic, failure of trains to connect at points of junction, delay of trains, any block at or between any station or stations, any· breakdown of plant or machinery, error of jUdgment or default of agents or workmen, however arising,. and tickets are sold subject to these conditions.
The right to stop the trains at any station or otherwise though not marked as a stopping place is J;eserved to the Department.
PASSENGERS FARES.
5. The fares for passengers shall be determined according to· the provisions of regulations 22, 26, 29 and 30. In the determination of the said fares, fractions of a mile shall count as a mile, fractions of a cent shall count as a cent, the minimum fare for a single, weekly, or season ticket shalloe that appropriateior a distance aX 4 miles, and the minimum fare for a return ticket (outwardaild inward) shall be that appropriate for a distance of 8 miles. .
GRATUITIES.
6. Railway Servants are forbidden, under penalty of dismissal to ask fer, or re-ceive from the public any gratuity.
CAUTION TO PASSENGERS.
7. No passenger shall be permitted to travel on the roof, steps, foot-board:or platform of any carriage, or on the engine, or in the Guard's Van or any portion 6f any carriage not intended for the conveyance of passengers nor will they be permitted to jump on or off the train whilst in motion and any passenger persisting in doing so, after being warned to desist by the Guard in charge of the train, or any duly autho-rised servant of the Government is subject to a penalty of $10.00, and shall be liable to be summarily removed from the Government premises.
SALE OF TICKETS ..
8. (a) The Ticket Booking Offices are opened for the issue of tickets half-an-hour ~before and may be closed one minute before the advertised time for departure of· trains except at Port-of-Spain where they are opened throughout the day.
(b) Passengers entraining at Halts, unless holding a season ticket, .return ticket, or pass, must travel in the coach provided for the purpose, and on entraining -must purchase their tickets from the Ticket-seller in that coach.
PASSENGERS TO EXAMINE TICKETS A':'fD CHANGE.
9. Passengers are requested to provide themselves with the correct fares as far as possible when taking tickets. Tickets and change (if any) should be carefully ·examined before leaving the Booking Office window, as mistakes cannot afterwards .be rectified.
PASSENGERS TO SHOW TICKETS.
10. No person shall, without the permission of a duly authorised Railway servant, 'enter any carriage on the Railway for the purpose of travelling therein as a passenger unless he has with him a proper Ticket or Pass. Every passenger by the Railway shall on the requisition of any Railway servant, present his Pass or Ticket to the Railway servant for examination, and at or near the end of the journey for which the Ticket, or in the case of a Season Ticket or Pass, at the expiration of the period for -which it is current, deliver up the Ticket or Pass to the proper Railway servant .
. TICKETS NOT TRANSFERABLE.
11. Tickets are not transferable and must not be wilfully altered or defaced so as io render the date, number or any material portion thereof illegible.
PASSENGERS TRAVELLING WITHOUT TICKETS.
12. If a passeiJ.ger 'travels in a train without having a proper Ticket or Pass with him, or being in or ha vin,g alighted from a train, fails or refuses to present for exami-nation or to deliver up his Ticket or Pass, immediately on requisition being made therefor, he shall be liable to pay on the demand of any Railway servant the penalty . ·charges hereinafter m,entioned, in addition. to the ordinary single fare for the.distance.
which he has travelled, or where there is any doubt as to the station from which he started, the ordinary single fare from' the station from which the train originally started. ' .
. First class 12 cent~ ..
Third class '6 cia..·
Provided that'such penalty charge shall in no case exceed the' amo-qnt of. the
,ordinary single fare which the passenger incurring the p'enalty charge is liable to pay~
. ' REBOOKI:N'G. .
13. Re-booking by the same train is strictly prohibited.
PARCEL OFFICES.
14. In Port-of-Spain the parcels office is opened daily for the receipt of pa,rcels at
·6.a.m. and for delivery of .parcels at8 a.m. and will be closed at 6p,m., except on Sundays, Good Friday, Christmas Dayand Corpus Christi. At country stations the ()ffices will be open both for receipt and delivery of parcels 20 minutes before the departure of the first train and will be closed after the arrival of the last train .
. 15. Passengers who have lupgage to book should 'b~ at the station at least' 15
mmutes before the advertised times for departure of trams, and at.the larger stations such longer time as may be necessary to permit of their luggage beiI?-g forwarded by the same train as that by which they intend to traveL
16. Passengers are only booked provided there is I-oom in the train by which,they
intend to travel; no overcrowding is permitted... - ' ..
40 (2)
17. Passengers will be booked and carried subject to the conditions of the RailwayS. Ordinance and to the Bye-la'ws of the Railway.
TICKET REFUNDS.
18. Passengers who may have lost or mislaid their tickets 'will be required to pay the ordinary fare, but should this ticket be afterwards found, application may be made to the General Manager of Railways for a refund of the amount. In the case of unused return halves of tickets a refund of the proper value may be allowed upon application being made to the General Manager of Railways (enclosing the ticket} within seven days of the date such ticket would ordinarily expire.
PLATFORM TICKETS.
19. For the convenience of passengers and to facilitate the business of the Railway it is found necessary to confine the use of the station platforms at Port-of-Spain. St. Joseph, Chaguanas and San Fernando to passengers arriving and departing by train. At such stations persons desirous of meeting or seeing off friends may be admitted by purchasing platform tickets, price 2 cents .. <Ftr~4ssue of these tickets may be limited or prohibited, as may be found desirous or necessary. Other stations may be closed similarly at the discretion of the General M,anager of Railways.
EXCURSION TRAINS.
20. Applications for excursions and special trains must be made to the General Manager of Railways. Seven clear days notice must be given. <;:harges for special trains are as follows:-
(a) For Excursion Trains the charge will be $2.00 per mile, outward and return mileage to be calculated, minimum charge $50.00. This charge will cover 400 Third class passengers, passengers above 400 will be charged pro rata. If First or other special coaches are required, these will be charged for at extra rates to be obtained from the General Manager of Railways.
(b) For Special Trains required to start from stations other than Port-of-Spain addItional charges for necessary light running may be made, particulars to be ob-tained from the General Manager of Railways.
(c) On Public Holidays, Fete Days, and on Special occasions the General Manager of, Railways, is ,empowered to grant reduced fares to the g~neral public. Every such Special Rate shall be reported forthwith to the Governor ..
(d) The granting or t'he refusing of any application for excursions or special trains, and the granting of reduced farestb the general public on,Pt;tb}ic Holidays, Fete Days or Special Occasions shall be ,in the sole discretion of the G-ep.eral Manager of Railways whose decision shall be fin3l. ' ' . ' '
21. Return halves of tickets shall be valid in the case of journeys of less than 16 miles ... for the date of issue and the day next following such date of issue, and in the case of journeys of 16 niiles or more, for seven consecutive days including the date of issue. '
(a) The General Manager of Railways is authorised to allow parties numbering not less than 12 full faTes to travel First class at Third class rates. S.uch parties must all travel together both going and returning. .
(b) When a guarantee of at least 50 persons is given the General Manager of Railways is authorised to grant a reduction which will amount to a single fare for the dOllbl~ journey. ' ,
(c) Nurses,- not exceeding twoin number, when in charge of invalids or chlldren; who are in possession of 1st class tickets may be allowed to travel 1st class. for 3rd, class fare. '
The above concessions are not granted without first obtaining the 'permission of the General Manager of Railways. '
CHILDREN UNDER 3 YEARS OF AGE.
22. (1) Every child under 3 years of age, if travelling in charge of a guardian an<;l not occupying a separate seat. will be carried free.
(2) Every child between 3 and 12 years of age will be charged one half the passenger fare ordinarily payable from time to time.
PERSONS TRAVELLING ON COMPLIMENTARY TICKETS OR FREE PASSES.
24. Persons travelling on a Complimentary Ticket or a Free Pass, in case of accident or loss of luggage cannot claim compensation of any kind from the Trinidad Government Railway.
REDUCED FARES FOR VOLUNTEERS, Boy SCOUTS, GIRL GUIDES, ETC.
25. Volunteers in uniform, Boy Scouts, Sea Scouts and Girl Guides, Children on educational visits, Orphan Home Bands-parties of-when attending Regimental Drills, Scout Rallies, Shooting Matches or Sports may travel at single fare for the double journey, provided they all go and return together.
A similar concession may be granted to groups other than the foregoing when on bona fide educational outings and approved as such by a District Inspector of Education.
MONTHL Y TICKETS.
26. (1) First and Third Class Monthly tickets shall be issued in accordance with this paragraph.
(2) Every such ticket shall be issued in respect of the period of one calendar month terminating on the last day of the month at the rate of 65 cents First Class and 45 cents Third Class per mile, calculated according to the mileage between the two stations specified thereon.
(3) No such ticket shall be issued in respect of journeys between two halts or· between a halt and a station.
(4) Every such ticket shall be valid during the calendar month specified thereon for sixty single continuous journeys between the stations specified thereon or between either of those stations and any intermediate station or halt, or between any two intermediate stations or halts.
(5) Monthly tickets may be issued to children travelling to and from school and to articled apprentices, at two-thirds the monthly ticket rates ordinarily payable from time to time. A further reduction of 20 per centum will be made in the rates· for children and articled apprentices upon all tickets-two in number or over-issued to bona fide members of the same family, at the same time, and covering the same period. In computing the said reductions, fractions of a cent shall count as a cent.
(6) A Monthly ticket shall not be valid unless:-
(a) the person for whose use it has been issued has signed the same with his
usual signature in English in ink or indelible pencil in the space provided for the purpose, or
(b) if such person cannot so sign his name, the officer of the railway issuing the
ticket has written the name of such person in the space provided for signature and has countersigned such writing.
27. Revoked.
REFUNDS IN RESPECT OF MONTHLY TICKETS.
28. (1) A refund may be obtained in respect of the unused portion of a Monthly Ticket on application to the Head Offices of the Railway, Port-of-Spain Railway Building, if all the following conditions are fulfilled:-
(a) . the application is made and the ticket is surrendered to the Railway
within the period for which the ticket is available;
(b) the application is made by the user of the ticket as evidenced by the
signature placed thereon at time of purchase;
(c) the application contains a statement over the signature of the applicant
of the reason why the refund is applied for;
(d) the ticket has not been maltreated, defaced or otherwise altered so as to
render any portion of the "lvTiting or printing thereon illegible.
(2) The amou?t to be refunded shall be ascertained as follows:-
(a) the number of trips used as evidenced by the ticket shall be multiplied by
the sum equal to the ordinary one way fare for the journey stated in the tick~t~
(b) the sum calculated above shall then 'be reduced by an amount equal to ten
per centum thereof; "
(c) should the sum so calculated in (a) and (b) above be greater than one-half
of the original purchase price of the ticket for which refund is claimed, there' shall be no refund payable;
(d) should the sum so calculated in (a) and (b) above be not greater than one-
half the original purchase price of the ticket for which refund is claimed, the difference between the amount so cakulated and the original purchase price of the ticket shall be reduced by an amount equal to twenty per cep.tum thereof and the remainder shall'be·the amount to be'refunded.
WEEKLY TICKETS.
29. (1) First and Third Class Weekly Tickets shall be issued in accordance with this paragraph.
(2) Every such ticket shall be issued in respect of any period of seven days at the rate of 25 cents First Class and 12t cents Thirq. Class per mile, calculated accord-ing to the mileage between the two stations specified thereon. "
(3) No such ticket shall be issued in r6dpect of journeys " between two ' halts or between a halt and a station.
(4) Every such t~cket shall be valid during the period of s~ven days specified thereon for fourteen single continuous journeys between the ",:, stations 'specified thereon or between either of those stations and any intermediat~'station or halt or between any two intermediate stations or halts. '
PASSENGERS FARES.
30. (1) The ordinary passenger single fare shall be at the rate of 5 cents per mne First Class, and if cents per mile Third Class for all distances, .
(2) The ordinary passenger return fare shall be at the same rateS but with a reduction of ten per centum on the rates ordinarily payable from time to time and in computing the said reduction fractions of a ce,!1t shall count as a cen~:, , ,
(3) Tickets are not transferable.
'PROVISIONS AS TO MONTHLY AND WEEKLY TICKETS. ' '
31. (1) Every user of a , Monthly or Weekly " Ticket shall in respect:.Qfeac;h ~. jow.ney made by him present his ticket to the proper officer of the Rail~ay f0r the:purp.ose of having the same punched or nipped by such officer. . ,
(2) Whenever any Monthly ,or Weekly Ticket which has been issued for the use of any person is presented for passage to pny officer of the Railway by any other person, such ticket shall be seized by such officer and except for g.ood cau,se shown to' the General Manager shall be forfeited. " ". ," . . "
(3) Any person who fails to comply with' the provisions o~ paragraph (1) hereof or who presents to an officer of the Railway a ticket not issued for his'use ~~all, be guilty of an offence and shan be 1iab~e on summary conviction to a fine of forty- .eight dollars. ' . .
EXCESS LUGGAGJ!: .•
32. (1) Passengers may have their luggage conveyed qn the" train on which th~y are travelling subject to the provisions of this regulation and to the provisions of regulation 33." , , - : ' •
(2) A passenger who intends to have his luggage conveyed on the train on which he is travelling shall tender all such luggage to be weighed by the proper Rail'way Ofiicer: Provided that luggage shall not be accepted at Halts where no regular staff is employed and that a passenger joining a train at such a Halt shall be entitled to take at his own risk in the carriage in which he is travelling only such hand luggage as will not cause inconvenience to any other passenger.
(3) All the luggage tendered by a passenger shall be weighed; and if the weight thereof be in excess of 100 lb. in the case of a first class passenger, in excess of 50 Jb. in the case of a third class passenger, a charge of 12 cents for each 50 lb. (or part thereof) in excess of such respective weights shall be made and payable for any distance.
(4) A receipt shall be delivered to it passenger 'whose luggage has been weighed and accepted for conveyance.
(5) Subject to the provisions of regulation 33 and of the proviso to sub-paragraph (2) of this regulation luggage shall be carried in the brake-van or other appointed place on a train and passengers must claim their luggage on arrival of the train at destination and deliver·the receipt thereof. .
K a responsibility will be accepted for loss or damage to luggage unless it has been properly addressed and secured and is cla.imed by the passenger on arrival at destination.
(6) The free allowances mentioned in sub-paragraph (3) of this paragraph shall be made only in the case of holders of ordinary tickets and, in the case of children paying half-fares only half such allowances will be made: Provided that there shall be no free allowance in respect of cycles, motor-cycles, perambulators, harmoniums or furniture conveyed as luggage.
(7) For the purposes of this paragraph and of regulation 33 the expression " luggage" of a passenger means articles belonging to the passenger and carried by him for his personal use and, convenience and includes groceries, provisions, and implements and tools used by him for the purposes of his trade or employ·ment. but does not include any bulky articles, explosives or dangerous goods.
SHUNTING CHARGES.
32A. A charge of 24 cents per ton shall be paid to the General Manager of Railways on all goods shunted by rail to and from Port-of-Spain Vlharves: Provided that the minimlCm charge shall be not .less than the sum chargeable for shunting 3 tons of goods.
LUGGAGE WITH OWNER.
33; (al Luggage or other articles, birds, properly caged, may be taken into carriages with owners if such can be done without causing inconvenience to passengers Should such luggage or other articles be liable for excess charges, the excess ticket issued will be remarked" V..r.O." meaning" with owner." The Eailway accepts no responsibility for luggage" with owner" and the excess luggage ticket handed to passengers in such cases is a: receipt for monies only, but the ticket must be surren-dered at destination station otherwise excess charge~ may be again collected ..
(b) The initials" O.R" mean" Owner's Risk" and indicate that the luggage or articles shown on the receipt are being conveyed at Owner's Risk.
. PARCELS BY PASSENGER TRAIN.
34. In each case parcels must be properly packed and addressed and should be brought to the Railway Station at least 20 minutes before the advertised time for departure of the train so as to ensure· despatch.
For distances up For distances
to and in- over
eluding 30 miles. 30 miles.
Parcels not exceeding 25 lb. in weight 12 cents 18 cents
Parcels over 25 lb. but not exceeding 50 lb. 24 do. 30 dr>.
Do. 50 lb. do. 100 lb. 36 do. 42 GO Do. 100 lb. do. 200 lb. 54 do. 60 do. Parcels exceeding 20.0. lb. in weight will be charged at the higher rates according to dlstance plus 24 cen~s for every additional 50. lb. or part of 50. lb. Parcels exceeding 4D~ lb. m welght wlll not be accepted for conveyance by Passenger Train. Un-dehvered parcels returned at half rates.
SPECIE.
35. Specie will not be accepted for conveyance by train, but when accompanied by passengers (at the entire risk of the owner), the charge will be the ordinary excess luggage rate, the usual luggage allowance being made.
RECEIPT OF PARCELS.
36. (a) Every consignments when tendered for carriage must be accompanied by a Consignment Note, signed by the sender or senders, or on his or their behalf, stating the name and address of the Consignee, the number of articles, and the weight and description of the goods, and other information as set out in such Consignment Note.
(b) Printed copies of the prescribed Consignment Note, which alone must be used, c~n be had at all stations on application.
(e) Signatures printed by a rubber stamp will riot be accepted ..
LEFT LUGGAGE.
37. Passengers may leave small packages of luggage and parcels in care of Station Masters at the various stations. A charge of two cents per day, or part of a day, will be made for each package. A receipt must in all cases be Gbtained, and packages so left will only be delivered to the person presenting such receipt, and paying any further charges that may be due. All packages thus left, if unclaimed, will be dealt with in accordance with regulation 19 under the heading" Passengers,Parcels and Goods."
ICE ..
38. Ice will be conveyed by Passenger Train at the :rate of six cents. per 10.0. Ib;· to include weight of package or part of 10.0 lb. up to 18 miles and ten cents per 10q ~b. or part of a 10.0 lb. for distances beyond,minimum six cents. Packages exceeding 40.0 lb. in weight will not be accepted for conveyance. Provided they are returned within one week from the date of despatch and from the original Consignee .to the original Consignur empties will be carried at half the full rates with a minimum of six cents.
ICE... "
(In wagon loads by Passenge1' Train.)·: ..
39. Ice in wagon loads will be conveyed by PassengerTraib. at thefollo~ingrates per six-ton truck (minimum) no rebate will be given on shipments of 5Qo. tons and over per annum...
1 mile to 10 miles .. $1.0.7 per tall. '. "
11 do. 20. do. 1.24 . do. .
21 do. 30 do. 1.40. do.
31 do. 40 do. 1.57 do:.
41 do. 50. do. 1.74 do.
51 do. 60 do. 1.90. do.
61 do. 70 do. 2.0.7 do.
MILK.
40. Milk in bottles or tins (at owner's risk) will be conveyed at a uniform rate·· of five cents for each 50 lb. or part of 50 lb. np to 3Q miles and nine cents for each 50. lb. or part of 50. lb. for distances beyond.
RETURN EMPTIES.
41. Return empties, may be carried free provided they are sent back within one week from date of despatch, and from the original Consignee to the original Consignor.
The above concession can be granted only by the General Manager of Railways.
BICYCLES, TRICYCLES, PERAMBULATORS.
42. If accompanied by passengers, bicycles, tricycles and perambulators will be carried at a uniform rate of 12 cents each, at owner's risk for any distance up to 30 miles, for distances over 30 miles the charge will be 18 cents. If unaccompanied by passengers double the above rate will be charged. Passengers wishing to carry more than one of the foregoing will have to pay double rates for all in excess of one.
(a) Season ticket holders will be granted the following rates for the conveyance of their bicycles when accompanying them:-
One month $1.80
Three months ... 4.35
Six months 7.98
To run concurrently with season ticket.
(b) Passengers' Bicycles may be left at Stations in care of Station Masters on payment of 2 cents per day, or 6 cents per week.
MOTOR CYCLES AND SIDE CARS.
43. Motor cycles conveyed by Passenger Trains will be charged 24 cents per 100 lb. up to 30 miles and 30 cents per 100 lb. for distances beyond with a minimum of 48 cents.
Motor cycles and side cars conveyed by Passenger Trains will be charged 36 cents per 100 lb. up to 30 miles and 42 cents per 100 lb. for distances beyond with a minimum of 72 cents. They will only be accepted if room is available.
Petrol tanks must be emptied before being brought on to Railway premises.
LIBRARY BOOKS, NEWAPAPERS, LETTERS, ETC.
44. (a) Letters containing money 'will not be accepted for conveyance by Railway, and the Railway accepts letters for conveyance at the rates shown below on this understanding.
(b) Letters and parcels of daily news not exceeding 15 lb. in weight will be subject to a uniform charge of eight cents for any distance, and where delivery is necessary the charge will be as follows :-12 cents for any distance within half a mile and 12 cents for every additional t mile beyond.
(c) Library books, four cents each consignment not exceeding 8 lb., minimum lour cents.
Special rates will be quoted to newspaper offices making regular despatches to the country.
FAMILY PROVISION BASKETS.
45. Family provision baskets, if properly packed so as noLto interfere with other articles and fully addressed; will be carried at the rate of 12 cents for 30 lb. or part of 30 lb. for any distance and 6 cents for each additional 30 lb. or part of 30 lb., return -empties free. Season ticket holders may purchase family provision basket tickets to run concurrently with their season tickets at the following rates:-
up to 30 lb. 30 to 50 lb.
One month ... $1.25 $2.00
Three months 2.50 4.50
Six months ... 5.00 8.50
Nine months 7.50 12.50
BREAKFAST BASKET TICKETS.
(.Lllaximttm weight 10 lb.)
46. Breakfast baskets will be carried at the rate of 8 cents for any distanee.: .. Season Ticket holders may purchase Breakfast basket tickets to run conc;u.rrentlv with their Season Tickets, at the followirig rates;"':" Return empties free. . .
Any distance.
One month $1.00
Three months 2.00
Six month's 4.50
Nine months 7.00
Breakfast Basket Season Tickets may be obtained by other than Season Ticket holders at the rate of $1.50 per month any distance.
Weight in excess of the maximum allowance 10 lb. will be charged at family provision basket rate.
MINERAL "VATER IN BOXES.
(At owner's risk.)
47. Mineral water in boxes will be charged at the rate of 18~cerits per 100 lb. up to 30 miles and 24 cents for distances beyond. Return empties half the above rates with a minimum of 12 cents.
GROUND PROVISIONS, FRUIT, FISH, EGGs.
48. Ground provisions, fresh fruit of all description, fish, firewood, eggs, industrial plants, charcoal, cocoa pods, oysters, moss, green fodder in small quantities, and local agricultural produce, if waybilled separately will be conveyed by passenger train at the rate of 12 cents per 100 lb. for any distance at owner's risk, aI).dthe minimum' charge therefor shall be 12 cents. If accompanied by passengers, an allowance of 50 lb. per passenger will be made, but any quantity in excess of that weight must be paid for in accordance with the above rates, with a minimum charge of 12 cents.
PIANOS,. HARMONIUMS, AMERICAN ORGANS.
49. When required to be forw~rdedby Passenger Tmiri, ~ia.Il~inoriiums;· and American organs wlll be 'charged as follows, at owner's risk.

MOTOR CARS, CARRIAGES, BUGGIES, . CARTS. .
.50. Will be carried by Passenger Train at the following rates at owner's risk':~

CORPSE, COFFIN, REFRIGERATOR, HEARSE.
51. _-"c corpse (in coffin or refrigerator) adult will be carried in a separate vehicle at the rate of 24 cents a mile, with a minimum charge of $2.40 (half rates for child's corpse) the attendant paying ordinary fare according to class.
Empty coffins 'will be carried at the rate of four cents a mile with a minimum charge of 48 cents.
Empty refrigerators will be carried at the rate of eight cents a mile with a minimum charge of 96 cents.
A hearse (with or without corpse) willbe carried at the rate of 24 cents a mile with a minimum charge of $4.80.
LIVE STOCK.
52. All li,-e stock is conveyed at owner's risk only, and owners must make their own arrangement for watering and feeding while on Railway premises and during transit.
53. Live stock not claimed by or on behalf of the owners on arrival at destination will remain in possession of the Railway entirely at the risk of the owners, and subject to any charge for feeding, watering or other necessary service, or such animal may be handed over to a Livery stable keeper at the risk and expense of the owner.
POULTRY.
54. Poultry, fowls, chickens with mother (two to count as one fowl), ducks, pigeons and guinea ·birds, etc., when accompanied by passengers, ,vill be carried at the rate two cents each. If waybilled separately double this rate will apply. No allowance for poultry will be made when forming part of a passenger's lnggage. If sent in crates, may be charged at parcels rate for total weight of crate and fowls if such charge be higher than the above quoted rates. Turkeys, geese, large birds, parrots and small turtle will be charged at a standing rate of six cents each for any distance, provided the owner travels by the same train. If waybilled separately double this rate will apply. Under no condition will.poultry be accepted for transit, with their wings locked or their feet tied.
DOGS.
55. Provided they are properly chained (and muzzled, if necessary) dogs will be carried in the dog-lockers of the Brake vans of Passenger trains at owner's risk ata rate of 12 cents per head for distances up to and including 30 miles, for distap.ces. beyond 30 miles and up to and including 45 miles 18 cents, beyond 45 miles f!.4 cents, provided the owner travels by the same train ... H waybilled separately double this rate. 'will apply. No dog will be accepted for transit without a collar and chain .. Persons sending dogs are reqnired to attach to each animal's collar a label:having the owner's and consignee's name and destination otherwise they' 'will not be accepted for transit.
PUPPIES, CATS, SUCKING PI,GS (SMALL), ETC.
56. Puppies, cats, kittens, kids, deer (small), rabbits, monkeys, morocoys, sucking pigs, caged birds, parrots and similar birds properly packed or crated when necessary, etc., will be charged at six cents the journey at .owner's risk (any distance), provided the owner travels by the same train. If waybilled separately donble this rate will apply. .
GOATS, SHEEP, PIGS, DEER (LARGE).
57. Goats, sheep, pigs, deer (large) will only be carried by Passenger trains where there is accommodation available, at the following rates, provided the owner travels by the same train:-
1 to 10 miles 12 cents per head.
11 ,,20 do. 18' do. 21 ,,30 do. 24 do. 31 ,,40 do. 30 do. Over 40 . do. 36 do.
H waybilled separately double this rate will apply.
ANIMALS REQUIRING EXCLUSIVE USE OF A VEHICLE.
58. Where an animal of any description cannot be carried in the Brake Van or other par~ially loaded truck and requires the exclusive use of a vehicle, the minimum charge WIll be $3.60.
ANIMALS IN PASSENGER CARRIAGES.
59. No animals will b~ allowed to travel in the Passenger Carriages.
ORDERS FOR HORSE BOXES AND OTHER VEHICLES.
60. Orders for horse boxes and other vehicles should be made in writing 24 hours in advance, to ensure their being in readiness. .
LOADING OF ANIMALS.
61. Horses, mules, cattle, donkeys, foals and calves (under 12 months of age) and other animals intended for conveyance by any particular train should be placed in the vehicle at least 20 minutes before the advertised time of departure.
WHEN A VEHICLE IS ORDERED BUT NOT USED.
62. vVhen a horse box or cattle truck is ordered but not used on the day specified the minimum charge of $2.40 may be enforced.
DELIVERY OF ANIMALS •
. 63. Delivery of animals will only be made at the particular station to which they are waybilled.
HORSES AND MULES TO BE PROPERLY HALTERED AND LABELLED.
64. Ko animal of the horse class will be received for conveyance unless properly' haltered and labelled, and must be loaded in the presence and und~r the 'charge of an attendant on the owner'sbehal£, who must travel through to . destination in charge of it, rendering any assistance that may bl:" required to 'the Railway ~taff in connection with it on route or at destination.' .
ATTENDANTS WITH ANIMALS.
65. Attendants travelling in the same vehicle with their animal do so at their own risk and will be charged Third class fare. . '
If Consignors elect to forward animals without an attendant they will. be reqUired to sign the following declaration:- .
" In consideration of the Railway agreeing to waive th.eir requirement of an
" attendant travelling with this animal and in charge of it, I hereby agree that " it so travels at my sole request, risk and responsibility, in respect of receipt, " travelling, delivery and of misdelivery." .
FODDER AND EQUIPMENT WITH ANIMALS.
66. The amount of Fodder and Equipment to be carried with 'an animal will be. limited to 150 lb., any weight in excess to be charged at the excess luggage rate.
HORSES, COWS, MULES, DONKEYS, FOALS AND CALVES.
67. Horses, cows, mules, donkeys, foals and calves (under 12 months old) will be conveyed by Passenger Train at the following rates. Minimum $3.60:---:-

GENERAL.
Railway.
PASSENGERS, PARCELS AND GOODS.
1. In computing rates all goo~s will be charged for at ton rates or the equivalent
1,000 lb. rate shmvn on pages 658,659. The decimal system will be used; fractions of a lb. will be charged as a lb., and fractions of a mile will be charged as amile. Decimals of a cent appearing in the . total charge raised . will count as a cent.
THROUGH TRAFFIC.
2. The General Manager of Railways is empowered to quote through rates ior all
classes of goods, to include the collection and delivery of such goods to and from Railway Stations. No such through rate sh8.11 be less than the sum of the Ancillary and Railway Charges calculated in accordance with the tariff.
CHANGES IN RATES AND FREIGHTS.
3. All applications for information as to changes in rates and freights, etc., should
be addressed to the General Manager of Railways, Railway Station, Port-at-Spain ..
CARRIAGE OF ARTICLE OF VALUE.
4. The Railway will not be liable for the loss of or injury to any article or articles, or property of the description following, that is to say, viz,: Gold or silver coin, or any gold or silver in a manufactured or unmanufactured state, or any precious stones, jewellery, watches, clocks, or time-pieces of any description, musical instruments, sewing machines, trinkets, bills, books of .account, stamps, maps, writings, title-deeds, printings, engrossings, pictures, sculpture or works of art,. gold or silver, plate or . plated articles, glass, china, silks in a manufactured or unmanufactur.ed state, and. whether wrought up or not wrought up with other material," furs, or lace, or any of them contained in any parcel or package which shall have been delivered either to be carried for hire or accompanying the person of any passenger when the value of such article or' articles, or property aforesaid contained in: ,such parcel or package shall exceed the· sum of $24.00, unless at the· time of delivery thereof to the Statioh Master for the purpose of being carried, or of accqmpanying the· person. of any passenger as .aforesaid, the value and nature of suchartide or~:rticles or property shall have been declared by the person or persons sending or delivering 'or carrying" the same, and such increased charge as hereinafter,mentioned shall have been paid to and accepted by some person duly authorised to receive the same on behalf oLtbe Railway Department. And the Railway Department hereby gives notice that for any parcel or package containing any of the articles above-mentioned, when"the .:value of such article or .. articles or property ,aforesaid contained in such parcel or . package shall exceed the sum of $24.00, an additiona,l charge of five per cent. on'the declared value thereof is required to be paid' over and above the .ordinary rate of carriage as a compensation for the greater risk incurred in the conveyance of such valuable articles. .
SPECIAL RATES.
5. The General Manager of Railways is empowered to grant Special Rates for any class of goods traffic, occasional, seasonaiorcontinuous, to any consignor who makes a specific contract with the General Manager of Railways to transport the whole of" his goods by rail or for exceptional consignments or in extraordinary circumstances: or to any consignor of goods exceeding a total of 500 tons per annum. Every such' Special Rate shall be reported forthwith to the Governor. .
Where special rates are quoted, they supersede the charge under the classification for the articles concerned, whether the class rates are lower or higher than the special rates, but remain subject to the Rules and Regulations and conditions laid down in the tariff.
CLAIMS.
6. All claims against the Government Railway for losE! or damage incurred in respect orany consignment of Goods or animals mus,tbe ma'ch~' to the Station Master jn charge of the station to which they have been booked before delivery or removal from the Railway premises of the property or of the package or packages, the con-tents or part of the contents of which are alleged by the claimant to be lost, or of the rest of the consignment, a portion of which is alleged by the claimant to be lost, as the case may be,' and also written statement of the nature of the damage sustained, .or in the case of loss full particulars of articles missing, must be forwarded to the General Manager of Railways, Railway Buildings, Port-of-Spain, within 24 hours after such consignment has arrived at its destination, otherwise the Government Railway will not hold itself responsible.
EXPLOSIVES.
7. The Railway Department gives notice that it is not a common carrier of ex-plosives or dangerous goods and that it does not undertake the carriage of any explosives except subject to the conditions set out in Explosives and Dangerous Goods Regulations.
Explosives will on no account be accepted by Passenger train.
DANGEROUS Goops.
8. No goods of a dangerous, combustible or explosive nature, or liable by leakage, exhalation, fermentation or similar process., to damage other goods in transit, to be packed with goods or sundries, but must be separately packed, clearly marked and 'described and due notice given to the sending station at the time of booking.
The station to enter such, goods on a separate waybill clearly marked" Dangerous " .all such packages to be separately . stowed and placed.
The Railway reserves the right to refuse to convey explosives and other ,dangerous consignments.
RIGHTS RESERVED TO CORRECT CHARGES.
9. The weight and description of goods as given in the Railway Invoices are inserted for the purpose of estimating freight.
The Railway does not admit that the actual weight as therein 'shown has been received, or that the description of the goods, as furnished by Consignees is correct, and therefore reserves the right of re-weighmellt, re-classification and re~calculation-' 'of charges before delivery.
- '
. PRE~PAYMENT . OF FREIGHT. . ' ,
10. The carriage of all goods must be prepaid, but a limited extension maybe allowed by arrangement with the General Manager of Railways in the case of the larger estates, and Merchants or others carrying business with the Government Railway; The Raitway, however, claims the right of withholding delivery .of any -consignment until the freight in respect of same be paid, or in satisfaction of any tolls .<fue upon a previous consignment or consignments,
CHEQUES.
11. Cheques (uncertified) will not be accepted in any payments made to the _ Railway by the public except by special arrangement with the General Manager of. Railway's.
PERISHABLE GOODS. -
12. Goods of a perishable nature are conveyed at owner's risk only, and are accepted for despatch on the understanding that the Railway is not held responsible for any detention or loss, from delay to trains and steamer or other unforeseen -circumstances.
The Railway does not accept any responsibility in the case of molasses, sugar and
rum for loss through defective package, fermentation, heating or other uncontrollable cause, nor for the detention of rum failing production of Customs Permit.
LIEN.
13. (a) If a person fails to pay on demand made by or on behalf of a Railway
Administration any rate, terminal, or other charge due from him in respect of any animals or goods, the Railway Administration may detain the whole or any of the goods, or if they have been removed from the Railway, any other animals or goods of such person then being in or thereafter coming into his possession.
(b) When any animals or goods have been detained under paragraph (a) the
Railway Administration may sell by public auction, in the case of perishable goods at once, and in the case of other goods or of animals on the expiration of at least 15 days notice of the intended auction published in the Royal Gazette or in one or more local newspapers sufficient of such animals or goods to produce a sum equal to the charge and all expenses of such detention, notice and sale, including in the case of animals the expense of the feeding, watching and tending thereof. .
(c) Out of the proceeds of the sale the Railway Administration may retain a
sum, equal to the chuge and the expenses aforesaid, rendering the surplus, if any, of the proceeds, and'Ttich' of the animals or goods, if any, as remain unsold, to the person entitled thereto.
(d) If a person on whom a. demand for any rate, terminal or other charge due
from him has been made fails to remove from the Railway within a reasonable time, any animals or goods which have been detained under paragraph (a) or anyanimals or goods which have remained unsold after a sale under paragraph (b) the Railway Administration may sell the whole of them and dispose of the proceeds of the sale as nearly as may be under the provisions of paragraph (cJ.
(e) Notwithstanding anything in the foregoing paragraphs the Railway
Administration may recover by suit any such rate, terminal, or other charge as aforesaid or balance thereof.
(f) When any animals have come into the possession of the Railway for carnage or otherwise, and are not claimed by the owner or other person appearing to the Railway Administration to be entitled thereto, the Railway Administration shall, if such owner or person is known, cause a notice to be served upon him, re-quiring him to remove the animals. .
(g) If such person or owner is not known, or the notice cannot be served upon him, or he does not comply with the requisition in the notice, the Railway Admimstra- . bon may within .a reasonable time, sell the animals as nearly as may be. under the provisions of paragraph (b) rendering the surplus, if any, of the proceeds of the salf' to any person entitled thereto.' .
OWNERS RISK. •
14. The Railway shall not be liable for the loss, injury, deterioration, or destruction of. goods or animals carried or delivered for carriage by the Railwayunless goods or animals in respect of which compensation is claimed, shall have been 'booked and' paid for in conformity with the rules and regulations in that behalf provided.
No LIABILITY FOR LOSS, INJURY OR DESTRUCTION OF A..'nMALs~
15. The Railway accepts no responsibility whatever in,case of death or accidtlnt to' any animal during transit or when being loaded into at unloaded from a' vehicle, or steamer or while waiting on Railway premises under the care of its attendant, no:: in· case the animal is left on the hands of the Railway Staff unattended for any cause nor for delivery or misdelivery, entanglement in its halter, or proper vice in the animal or consequences arising therefrom, nor in the event of the owner failing'to- send a. groom to take charge to its destination, nor in any other circumstances except short ,delivery.
16. The Railway ,vill accept no liability for any accident or injury which may result to any animal while under the care or in charge of the agents or servants of the sender or consignee on Railway premises, even if the formality of the signing of the document has not at the moment been completed.
17. In case of short delivery, the Government will only be responsible to the amount of $48.00 for a horse or a mule, $38.40 for a cow, bull or bullock, $19.20 for an ass or calf, $4.80 for a sheep or pig, $1.20 for a goat and 72 cents for a fowl, unless the value of the animal or bird has been declared, and an insurance of two per cent., on the difference between the declared value and the above Railway valuation paid.
No LIABILITY FOR I:ti']URY TO ATTENDANT.
18. The Railway will accept no liability for injury of any kind caused by an animal to its attendant either on the Railway premises or during transit.
UNCLAIMED.
19. Live stock not claimed by or on behalf of the owners on arrival at destination will remain in possession of the Railway entirely at the risk of the owner, and subject to any charge for feeding, watering or other necessary service or snch animal may be handed over to a Livery stable-keeper at the risk and expense of the owner.
(a) Unclaimed articles of all descriptions (except perishable goods) will be kept at stations or depots for four days and will then be sent to the Claims Department, Port-of-Spain, to be kept for a further period of three months aft.er which period they will be sold by Pnblic Auction, after due notice of such sale has been given.
(b) Unclaimed perishable articles may be disposed of at the station where found, at the discretion of the Railway.
(e) Any amounts received for the sale of unclaimed articles may be refullded to persons proving their ownership of such articles, after deduction of any sums which may be due to the Railway in respect thereof.
GOODS TARIFF.
General Rules and Conditions for the Carriage of Goods Traffic:
1. (a) The Railway will not be accountable for any articles unless the same be signed fa): and received by its duly authorised Agents or Clerks.
(b) Nor will it be responsible for g~~ds left on its premise~ for the convenience of the sender; except as ·hereinafter provided.
(e) The transit will be deemed to have terminated 12 working hours after the goodshave been placed at the Consignee's disposal. After that time the goods will be at the owner's sale risk. Goods warehoused by the Railway, i.e., goods on which storage charges may accrue will be at the o'wner's risk as regards damage or deterioration. .
(d) Nor wili it be liable for any loss of, or damage to goods from the act of God, fire or civil commotion, nor for the loss of or damage to goods put up in returned wrappers, or boxes, or packages described as " empties" or for any goods left until called for, or to order, nor for the loss of any packages or their contents which may be insufficiently, improperly, or incorrectly packed, marked, directed, or described: nor for any damage to any such packages or their contents or to any package which contains a variety of articles liable to break, or to damage each other: nor for leakage arising from bad casks or cooperage, bad soldering or defective joints in tins and drum, or for any loss of or damage to any goods whatever, by reason of accidenta.l or unavoidable delays in transit or otherwise.
(e) Nor will it, in the case of explosive or dangerous goods be liable for any loss, destruction or damage caused through loading,. stowage, or unloading; nor for discrepancy in the delivery as to either quantity, number, or weight nor for the -condition of articles so carried, nor for any consequence arising from over carriage, detention, or delay in, or in relation to the carriage and delivery thereof.
To-VIII. 41 2. The Railway does not guarantee the despatch of goods by any particular train neither will it be responsible for delivery at destination by any specified date of time. .
RECEIPT OF GOODS.
3. (a) Every consignment of goods, when tendered for carriage nmst be accom-panied by a Consignment Note, signed by the Sender or Senders, or on his or their behalf, stating the name and address of the Consignee, the number of articles, and the weight and description of the goods, and other information as set out in such Consign-ment )rote.
(b) Printed copies of the prescribed Consignment Note, which alone must be used, can be had at all stations, on application.
(el Signatures printed by a rubber stamp will not be accepted.
4. If an Owner, Consignor, or Consignee give an account in respect of the descrip-tion of goods which is materially false the Railway will charge arate which is double the highest existing class rate on the Railway.
S. The Railway authorities shall have the right to detain and examine the contents of any package or consignment tendered for despatch, or which has been carried by the Railway.
tal If the actual Consignee does not apply in person for the consignment he will be required to sign the Advice Note referred to in the second paragraph of regulation 7 below authorising the Railway to deliver to bearer, and consignments cannot be delivered unless this is done.
(b) The Inward Delivery Book must be signed by the Consignee or his Agent, or alternatively the signature of Consignee or his Agent will be taken OD the Railway Invoice, and who will be permitted at the time of delivery to make a remark as to the nature of the loss or damage if any.
(e) In the case of illiterates, a thumb impression will be taken as a signature, the name of the illiterate being written in the delivery book by the delivery clerk.
PERISHABLE GOODS AND FRAIL ARTICLES.
6. Perishable Goods and Frail Articles will only be ac~epted at.the owner's risk: .
DELIVERY OF GOODS. . .
7. Excepting at Port-of-Spain Goods Offices are open for the receipt and delivery of merchandise and all goods from 7 a.m. to 5 p.Hl. daily, with the eXGeptioil of Sundays, Cill;istmas Day, Good Friday, Corpus Christi, or other days dedar.ed ~s Public Holidays, when all Railway Offices are closed. AtPort-of-Spai:nthe Goods Sheds will be opened from 7 a.m. to 4.30 p.m., Mondays to Fridays indusive, 'and on Saturdays 7 a,m. to 1 p.m. Inwards Goods will be delivered from 8.30 a.lll,
The delivery of all goods will be considered complete if a ·notice of their: ·arriv3] is given to the Consignee, or posted to him, or left any time between '7 a.m. and 5 p.m: on any working day at his usual or last known place of abode or business;·, but non-rece~pt of such advice will not entitle the Consignee to exemption from demurrage. If delivery is not taken within 48 hours after arrival, the goods. ·will remain at the risk and experise of the Owner or Consignee. .
The Railway does not undertake the delivery of goods of any des,cription within any specified time, unless special arrangements are made .. with the officer in charge at the time of despatching, nor in the event of accident to any part of the train, landslips, or other uncontrollable event. .
In all cases where the identity of the person claiming the goods is unknown, the :R~il"Way also reserves the right to demand, prior to delivery, an undertaking in ·writihg to indemnify the Railway from all claims in respect of the goods.
Goods not specified in the classification are charged at the highest class rate O'r upon such terms as may be specially arranged with the General J\:Ianager of Ra.il",·ays.
Consignments of goods, the contents of which are not declared in writing through· l1eglect or refusal on the part of the senders, or which cannot be clearly described. charged at the highest class rate.
,./
The Railway ,yill accept no responsibility as to loss of or damage to goods of any description unless the same be brought to the notice of a responsible officer of the Railway within four hours after the truck has been delivered into the private siding -or unloading place, nor in a.ny case where the delivery is made after 5 p.m.
LOADING AXD UNLOADING.
8. (a) Loading and unloading will not be permitted on the main line behveen 'Stations, excepting under special arrangement" to be made with the General Manager dRailways.
(b) Rates include loading and unloading except where specified otherwise in the .classification of goods, in which case the actual cost of loading and unloading will be .charged when done by the Railway.
(c) All classes of goods loaded and unloaded at private sidings will be done by sender and consignee at their cost.
LIABILITY FOR UNBOOKED GOODS.
9. No liability for loss, injury or damage to goods brought on the Railway premises for despatch can be admitted unless such goods have been accepted, and a printed Railway Receipt granted for them by a duly authorised Raihyay Servant: nor will the receipt be granted until all the conditions as required by these regulations are duly fulfilled, and where senders are required to load the consignments, until the -consignments are loaded.
CONSIGNJlIENTS OF DIFFERENT CLASSES OF GOODS.
10. In booking small consignments of different classes of goods of a less weight than 100 lb., the total weight of the ,vhole of the articles will be taken and charges raised on the rate for the highest class of goods contained in the consignment.
CHARGES ON DIFFERE:-<T PACKAGES AND WAGONS OF GOODS.
11. A package containing two or more classes of goods the weight of which cannot 'be separately ascertained, will be charged at the rate of the highest class of goods which the package contains.
PARTIAL DELIVERY OF A CO:-<SIGK~[ENT.
12. If Consignee refuses to take delivery of a consignment or part thereof because it is short or damaged, the goods refused will remain on the Railway preinises at the risk of the owners as regards damage or deterioration, and will be subject to the usual .charges for storage and demurrage.
MINIMUM CHARGE.
13. The minimum charges for any consignment will be 12 cents.
LIABILITY OF THE H.AILWAY FOR GOODS DEPOSITED UPQN WHARVES AND LOADED
FROU MERCHANTS SHEDS AND SIDINGS.
14. The Railway \vill not be responsible for the quantity or condition of goods loaded by the Consignors from merchants shed or siding, without having been tallied by the Railway.
(a) The Railway \,'ill not be responsible for either loss or damage in the case of ,goods deposited on the various open wharves, sidings or Local Halts awaiting .delivery or despatch by Railway, and not in the former cases until the full quantities according to Consignment Note shall have been duly checked, and a receipt given, by a responsible officer of the H.ailway Department.
(b) Traffic to and from sidings between stations n1.ay, in the_discretion of the General I1'Ianager of Railways, be charged at the same mileage rates as to or from the Goods Stations next beyond according to the direction of the traffic, plus any .additional distance of haulage rendered necessary through positions of fhe sidings.
41 (2)
FREIGHT PAYMENTS TO BE :MADE O:NLY TO AUTHORISED OFFICERS.
15. JHerchants and others sending or receiving goods by Railway, are requested not to pay any charges except those demanded by the recognised Goods Clerk, nor to, any person other than the Clerk appointed to receive money in the Goods Ofuce. Loading Clerks and Porters are paid by the Railway and should not receive any payment whatever from Merchants or the public.
MARKING OF GOODS.
16. All consignments of goods booked are required to be marked with the names: of the despatching and receiving stations and name and address of Co:rr.rignee, 'and the: Railway will not be responsible for non-delivery or mis-delivery arising from in-sufficiency, illegibility, or incorrectness of address.
RE-BoOKING.
17. (a) "Whenever goods or parcels lying undelivered at a station are required to' be; re-consigned to another, or sent back to the original forwarding station, the Consignor, or Consignee, is required to pay the charge due for the original consign-ment, as well as the charges for re-booking before removal of goods. This re-booking. will oniy be done on a written authority from either Sender or Consignor, and on production of the original Railway receipt, which must be surrendered before the goods are re-booked.
(b) The sum of the original and re-booking charges shall not be less than the-charge from the original station to the final destination exclusive of any demurrage or storage charges.
REFUND OF FREIGHT.
18. No person shall be entitled to a refund of overcharge of freight on animals or goods carried, unless his claim is preferred in writing to the General Manager of Railways within seven days from the date on which the animals or goods are de-livered by the Railway. No refund of a less sum than 24 cents on anyone consign-ment will be made. A deduction of ten per cent. (maximum 24 cents) may be made from all amounts refunded unless the overcharge has been caused by Departmental errors.
MACHINERY, BOILERS, BULKY ARTICLES.
19. (a) Boilers and heavy machinery will be carried under special arrangemcJ,lts to be made with the General Manager of Railways.
(0) The Railway will not accept for conveyance by rail:-
(i) Any article or package weighing more than three tons.
(ii) Any article or package in excess of the following dimensions.'
Length 12 feet. .
Height... 9dci ..
Width 8 do.
except b~T special arrangement with the General Manager of Railways.

TABLE OF CHARGES FOR CARRIAGE OF HEAVY GOODS.
21. The following additional charges will be made in the case of heavy goods;-
WHEN LOADED ur AT RAIL,VAY STATIONS.

ordinary tariff charge. $
If the truck contains one or more pieces between 1,400 lb. and 1,750 lb.
in weight, ordinary tariff charge plus 0.50

in weight, ordinary tariff charge plus 1.00

in weight, orC4nary tariff charge plus 1.50
"\VHEN LOADED UP BETWEEN RAILWAY STATIONS.
If the truck does not contain any piece weighing 1,400 lb. in weight,
ordinary tariff charge plus 1.50
If the truck contains one or more pieces between 1,400 lb. and 1,750 lb.
in weight, ordinary tariff charge plus 2.00

in weight, ordinary tariff charge plus 2.50

in weight, ordinary tariff charge plus 3.00
22. In cases where it is not possible for the Station Master to assure himself of the -weight of consignments loaded up between Stations, he must charge the proper rate .and insert a remark on the Waybill for the Receiving Station to have the weights ·checked and collect the extra freight due, and notify him of the particulars required -for the supplement Waybill.
The extra charge must be entered on the 'Waybill under the ordinary freight charge :as " Extra for Loading and Unloading."
23. The Railway reserves the right of carrying goods either by measurement, or by weight, as the Railway Administration may decide.
24. In the case of wagons 'which are given to Consignors at their request for loading, or wagons placed in private siding for loading, a minimum charge as for the marked capacity \vill be made. Should the actual weight of a consignment in a wagon <exceed the marked capacity such actual weight will be charged.
