STORAGE CHARGES.
26. All Goods left on the premises of the Railway whether in warehouses or other building or in the open for upwards of 48 hours after midnight of the day on which they are unloaded at destination, either for the convenience or by the desire or neglect of the Consignor or Consignee, are stored at Owner's risk as regards damage' or d.eterioration and may, as from the expiration of the said 48 hours, be subject to a warehonse rent or storage of three cents for every day or portion of a day fOl' every 100 lb. or part thereof.
27. Special arrangements may be made ,yith the General Manager of Railways. for the storage of such commodities passing in lots of not less than 500 tons per annu·ro. '
28. Goods brought to a station for despatch may be subject:-,
(a) If allOwed, either for the convenience or by the desire or neglect of the
Consignor, before despatch to remain whether in warehouses or other buildings or in the open, at Owner's risk as regards damage or deterioration for more than 48 hours from the time ,when the first portion of the consignment arrived at the station, to a warehouse rent or storage charge of three cents per day or portion of a day for every 100 lb. or part thereof, such charge to be calculated as from midnight of the day on which the first portion of the consignment was brought to the station, and only-on such portion of the consignment as has remained unhooked for 48 hours.
(b) If withdrawn without being despatched,to a ,varehouse rent or'stC!n1ge
charge at the aforesaid rate as from the hour when such goods were brought for despatch without any free allowance.
INSURANCE OF SUGAR.
29. All sugar for shipment must be covered by Owner's Insurance, free of cost to the Rail,Yay whilst lying in '\"1harf Sheds, on ViTharfs or in Lighters:
DEMURRAGE CHARGES.
30. (a) Demmrage at the rate of 24 cents per ton of truck capaCity per day oi.-portion of a day may be charged on every wagon loaded with goods unless such ,vagon has been discharged within 24 hours after beiilg placed in a position for unloading. (Sundays and Public Holidays excluded.) , , .
(b) 'When a wagon covered by a tarpaulin is placed in a private sidil1g lor unloading, demurrage at the rate of $1.20 per day or part of a day may be charged on the tarpaulin unless the tarpaulin is returned 'within 24 hours after tlie wagon \yas placed in the siding. (Sundays and Public Holidays excluded.)
31. (a) Demurrage at the rate of 24 cents·per ton of truck capacity per day or portion of a day may be charged on every wagon ordered and not loaded, or
.,/
improperly loaded, or loaded and not ready for despatch after 24 hours from the time \vhen the ''lagon was placed in position for loading. (Sundays and Public Holidays excluded.)
(b) Demurrage at the rate of $1.20 per day may be charged for a tarpaulin supplied to an Il,state or other customer for covering a wagon consigned over the Railway unless the wagon is handed over to the Railway for despatch within 24 hours of the tarpaulin being supplied. (Sundays and Public Holidays excluded.)
32. A charge of 24 cents per ton shall be paid to the General Manager of Railways on all goods shunted by rail to and from the Port-of-Spain V'lharves: Provided that the minimum charge shall be 24 cents.
UNCLAIMED GOODS.
33. (a) Unclaimed articles of all descriptions except perishable articles will be kept at stations for four days when they will be sent to the Claims Department, Port-of-Spain, to be kept for a further three months after which period they will be sold by Public Auction, after due notice of such sale has been given.
(b) Unclaimed perishable articles may be disposed of at the station where found at the discretiop. of the Railway. .
(c) Any amounts received for the sale of unclaimed articles will be refunded to persons proving their ownership of such articles after deduction of any sums which may be due to the Railway in respect thereof.

TRINIDAD GOVERNMENT RAILWAY.
Bye~laws for Regulating Passengers and General Traffic, made under section
18 of the Railways Ordinance.
1. (i) No passenger will be allowed to enter upon any platform of or into any carriage used on the Railway, or to travel upon the Railway, unless furnished with a ticket specifying the class of carriage and the Stations for conveyance between which such ticket is issued. Every passenger shall show and deliver up his ticket (whether a contract or season ticket, or otherwise) to.any duly authorised Servant of the Railway whenever required to do so for.any purpose. If a passenger travels· in a train without having a proper pass or ticket with him, or being in -or having alighted from a train, fails or refuses to present for examination or to deliver up his pass or ticket, immediately on requisition: being made therefor, he shall be liable'to pay on the demand of any Railway Servant the penalty charges hereinafter .men~ tioned, in addition to the ordinary single fare for the distance which he has .t.ravelled, or where there is any doubt as to the station from which he started, in addition to the ordinary sing,le fare from the station from which the tr~in originally st.arted:
First Class 12 cents .
. Third Class . _. 6 cents.
_ (ii) Provi.ded that such penalty charge shall in no case exceed the' amount 'of, the·ordinary single fare which the passenger incurring the penalty charge is liable to pay.
(iii) Any person refusing to pay the fare and penalty charge hereinbefore mentioned is subject to a penalty of $10.
2. Any passenger using or attempting to use a: ticket on any day for which such ticket is not available, or using or attempting to use a ticket which has been already used on' a previous journey, is subject to a penalty of $10 ..
3. Any passenger using or attempting to use a ticket for any other Station than that for which it is available, will be required to pay the difference between the sum actually paid and the single fare between the stations from and to which the passenger has travelled, or, at the option of the Railway Authorities, the single fare from the Station to which he was booked to the end of his journey. Any passenger who having paid his fare for a certain distance knowingly and wilfully proceeds beyond that distance without previously paying the additional fare for the additional distance, and v,rith intent to avoid payment thereof, shall be liable to a fine of $10, and in case of a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of $48. A ticket, whether single or return, is only available between the Stations marked thereon, and any passenger alighting at an intermediate Station will be required to forfeit and deliver up such ticket, and upon resuming the journey by any subsequent train, to take a fresh ticket. Re-booking by the same train is strictly disallowed. .,
4. Any passenger wilfully altering or defacing his ticket so as to render the date, number, or any material portion thereof illegible, is subject to a penalty of $24.
5. (i) Return tickets are not transferable.
(ii) The outward halves are available on the day of issue only, the return halves are available for 28 days from date of issue.
(iii) Return tickets are granted solely for the purpose of enabling the person to whom the same is issued to travel therewith to and from the Stations marked thereon. Any person who sells or attempts to sell, or parts or attempts to part with the posses-sion of the return half of any return ticket in order to enable any. other person to' . travel therewith is subject to a penalty of $24, and any person purchasing such half return ticket or travelling, or attempting to travel, therewith shall be liable to pay the fare which he would have been liable to pay for the single journey~ and shall, in : addition thereto, be subject to a penalty of $24.
6. Every person smoking in any shed or waiting room, or in any vehicle, in 'which smoking is prohibited, is subject to a penalty of $10. The Railway Officers and Ser-vants are required to take the necessary steps to enforce obedience to this Bye-law, and any person offending against it is liable, in addition to incurring the penalty above-mentioned, to be summarily removed, at the first opportunity, from the vehicle, or from the Railway premises.
7. No lighted cigar, cigarette or other burning substance shall be thrown or dropped from a train. Any person contravening this bye-law shall be subject to a penalty of $48.
8. Any person travelling, without the special permission of some duly authorised Servant of the Railway, in a carriage of a superior class to that for which his ticket is issued will be liable to pay the difference in the single fares according to the classof carriage in which he is travelling, from the Station whence the train originally started to destination, and unless he shows that he had no intention to defraud, shall, in addition be subject to a penalty of $10.
9. Any person founel in a carriage, or elsewhere upon the Railway premises in a state of intoxication, or using obscene language, or writing obscene or offensive words on any part of the Stations or carriages, or committing any nuisance, or wilfully interfering with the comfort of passengers, or otherwise misconducting himself, is subject to a penalty of $10, and may immediately, or if a passenger, at the first opportunity, be removed from the Railway premises.
10. Any person who wilfully cuts or tears ariy lining or window strap or curtain, removes or defaces any number plate, or breaks or scratches any window of a carriage used on .the Railway, Dcwho other-wiseiexceptbyunavoidable ..accident;···uamages, 'defaces or injures any such carriage, or any Station, or any other property of the' Government is subject to a penalty of $24, in addition to the amount of any damage ,for which he may be liable.
11. No passenger shall be permitted to travel on the roof, steps, foot-board or })latform of any carriage, or on the engine, or in the Guard's van or any portion of any carriage not inten'ded for the conveyance of passengers; and any passenger persisting in doing so after being warned to desist by the Guard in charge of the train, or any duly authorised Servant of the Railway, is su)Jject to a penalty of $10, and shall be liable to be summarily removed from the Railway premises.
12. (i) A person, not being an intending passenger, shall not without the consent -of a duly authorised servant of the Railway enter any station, or platform, or the approaches thereto or other railway premises, and no person, whether an intending passenger or not being on any station premises shall open, close or displace any door, gate, or barrier used for the preservation of order, or for the control of the entrance or exits, or pass by any passage or way to or from the platforms, o:(fices, waiting rooms or other premises of the Railway except as permitted by the officers in charge of such premises, or alight, or enter any train otherwise than at the appointed place and in the appointed manner.
(ii) Any person infringing this Bye-law will be liable to a penalty of $48.
13. (i) At Port-ai-Spain, San Fernando, St. Joseph and Chaguanas Stations, and such other stations as may from time to time be specifi,ed by the General Manager of Railways no person except intending passengers and persons otherwise duly autho-rised shall enter upon the platforms unless he has purchased at the booking office a platform ticket at a cost of two cents.
(ii) Such tickets shall remain the property of the Railway, and shall be surrendered by the holder either upon entering or leaving the platform as may be demanded by the authorised Railway Officer.
(iii) If the working of the Railway is likely to be retarded or inconvenienced by the issue of such tickets, such issue may be refused, or a limited number of tickets issued. . .
(iv) Any person infringing this Bye-law will be liable to a penalty of $48.
14. Any passenger entering or leaving, or attempting to enter or leave any carriage while the train is in motion, or elsewhere than at the place appointed for passengers to enter or leave the carriages, is subject to a penalty of $10.
15. Any passenger persisting in entering a carriage or compartment of a carriage containing the full number of persons which it is constructed to convey when any {)lie of such persons objects to his so entering the carriage or compartment, is subject to a penalty of $10.
16. Dogs and other animals will not be allowed to accompany passengers in the carriages; but will be conveyed separately and charged for, and any person taking a dog or other animal with him into any passenger carriage used on the Railway is subject to a penalty of $10, in addition to the usual rate for carriage of a dog.
17. Loaded fire-arms are on no account to be. taken into or placed upon any carriage, wagon, truck, or other vehicle forming, or intended to form, a train or any portion of a train on the Railway, or to be brought to a Station, or on to any premises of the Railway, and every person so offending is subject to a penalty of ~Z4.
18. The Railway may refuse to carry any person who has any infectious disease. If any person who has any such disorder be found upon the premises of the Railway, or travels, or attempts to travel on the Railway without the special permission of the General Manager, he shall be liable to a penalty of $24, in addition to the forfeiture of any fare which he may have paid, and may be removed at the first opportunity from the Railway premises. Any person who has charge of any person suffering from an infectious disease, while upon the premises of the Railway or travelling or attempt-ing to travel, on the Railway, or-who aids or assists any person suffering from such disease in being upon the premises of the Railway, or travelling or attempting to travel on the Railway, shall be liable to a penalty of $24, unless the pers'on suffering from such disease be travelling with the special permission of the General Manager of Railways.
19. Every driver of any carriage, buggy, cart, lorry, motor or other vehicle shall while in or upon any Station-yard, Wharf, or other premises of the Railway, obey the directions of the Railway Officers and Servants duly authorised on that behalf, and every person offending against this bye-law is subject to a penalty of $10, and may;. in addition to incurring the penalty above-mentioned, be summarily removed from the premises.
20. If any person leaves open any gate for a level crossing or any gate set up at either side of the Railway for the accommodation of the owners or occupiers of the adjoining lands, he shall be liable to a penalty of $10.
21. If any person shall be or pass upon the Railway except for the purpose of crossing the same at any authorised crossing, or shall trespass upon any of the Stations and premises connected with such Railway he shall be liable to a penalty of $10.
22. The Government will not be responsible for the value of any animals which may be killed or injured while trespassing on the Railway lands. Any poundable animals found trespassing on the Railway will be liable to be impounded, and the owner of any animal so trespassing shall be liable to a penalty of $24.
23. No person shall pass through, into, over or upon any train in motion or any part of such train, or through, into, or upon any part of the line or rails within such distance of a train in motion, that the passage is dangerous. Any person offending' against this Bye-law shall be liable to a penalty of $24.
24. Any person who shall attempt to carry or send by the Railway, gunpowder, lucifer, or Congreve matches, vitriol, ardent spirits,- paraffin, petroleum, kerosene, or other mineral oil, turpentine, naphtha, camphine or any article which in the judgment of the Railway authorities may be of an explosive or highly combustible nature~ 'without distinctly marking the nature of such articles on the case containing them, or otherwise; giving notice in writing to that effect tothe General Manager of Rail-ways or the Goods Clerk to whom they are delivered, will be liable to a fine of S48 for each offence; and if any person shall vvilfully fail to give true account of what he knows tD be the contents of any parcel or package sent by Railway, or shall wilfully give a false account of the same to any Servant of the Railway Department receiving such parcel or package, he shall for every such offence be liable to a fine of $24.
25. Any person who sells or attempts to sell any article in any carriage or elsewhere on the Railway premises without the written authority of the General Manager of Railways is subject to a penalty often dollars, and may immediately, or if a passenger, at the first opportunity be removed from the Railway premises.
BYE-LAWS.
Explosives and Dangerous Goods.
L These Bye-laws may be cited as the Railway (Carriage of Explosives) Bye-Iaws~ 2. The Railway Department gives notice that it is not a common carrier of explosives or dangerous goods and that it does not ·undertake the carriage of any' explosive except on special conditions signed by the sender thereof, or by the person delivering same to the department for carriage. . .
3. The word" explosive" includes:~
(1) Gunpowder, Nitro-glycerine, Dynamite, Gun-cotton, Blasting . powders,
Fulminate of mercury or of other metals, Coloured fires, and every other subsbmce whether similar to those above-mentioned or not, used or manu-factured with a view to produce a practical effect by explosion, or a pyrotechnic effect, and
(2) Fog-sigrials, fire-works, f:llses, rockets, percl\ssio'n caps, detonators,
cartridges, ammunition of all sorts, and every adaptation or preparation of an explosive as above defined.' .
The expression " dangerous goods" includes:- ! Any inflammable liquid, gas, or:substance liable to spontaneous combustion, or to give 6ff inflammable or poisonous vapour, or 'any harmful mixture' or preparation of the same~ .
Any corrosive or poisonous chemicals, and the return empty packages that ,have conveyed the same;
Any commodity of an inflammable nature or liable to chemical or mechanical change in course of transit or handling, due to combination with air or moisture, by heat, oscillation, compression, exhalation or other natural process likely to cause, or become capable of causing injury to persons or property, such as-
Acetone and Ketone Oils, Benzole, Naphthas, Toluol, Turpentine and Turpen-
tine substitutes, Preparations of Xylol, the lighter oils obtained from Petroleum, Anti-Fouling and Anti-Corrosive Compositions, Rubber Solution, Ether, Bisulphide of Carbon and all other inflammable liquids capable of giving off a vapour inflammable at less than 90 degrees Fah., Muriatic, Nitric and Sulphuric Acids, Potassium, Calcium, Sodium, Ammonia Solution (specific gravity 0.880 to 0.891), Chromic'Acid, Phosphorus, Arsenic, Carbolic Acid, Cyanide of Potas-sium, Bleaching liquid, Paint and Varnish removers, Tin solution, Electric accuml'\lators containing acids, the Nitrates of, Barium" Iron, Lead, Potash, Soda and Strontium, Matches, Oily goods, Compressed gases, Calcium carbide, Calcium phosphide, Holmes Patent Signal Lights, Patent Umd and Marine
Lights, Charcoal dust, Celluloid and ,oth(':r commodities of a like character. ,
4. Every package shall be fully addressed and shall bear thereon the name of the Consignee, his address, and station at which to be delivered plainly set out, and shall be so labelled or branded under the word" Explosive" or!' Dangerous "'as the case may be, as to clearly show the nature of the Explosive or Dangerous class of goods, and the division and class to which the contents belong.
Each consignment shall be accompanied by a special consignment note properly and completely filled up and signed by the sender. The special consignment note shall in every case be used.
5. Senders giving incorrect consignment notes, or otherwise attempting to forward any explosive or dangerous articles under improper declaration, or contrary to these Bye-laws, are in every such case liable to the penalty set forth in the Raihvay Ordinance.
6. Dangerous goods will, except otherwise provided, only be carried by the depart~ ment at owner's risk and under conditions set forth in the respective consignment notes contained in these Bye-laws and further, as regards explosives, in accordance with these Bye-laws.
7. (1) Explosives are classified as follows:-
(a) CLASS I.-Gunpowder.
(b) CLASS n.-Nitrate lI1'ixture-·meaning any preparation other than Gun-
powder ordinarily so called, formed by the mechanical mixture of a nitrate with any form of carbon or with any carbonaceous substance not possessed of explosive properties. '
(e) CLASS III.-Nitro-Compounds-meaning any chemical compound POS-
sessed of explosive properties, or capable of combining with metals to form an explosive conipound which is produced by the chemical action of nitric-acid (whether mL'l:ed or not with sl(lphuric acid)' or of a nitrate mixed with sulphuric acid, upon any carbonaceous substance whether such compound is mechanically mixed "'lith other substances or not.
(d) CLASS IV.-Chlorate-mixtures-meaning any explosive containing a
chlorate.
(e) CLASS V.-Fulminate-meaning any chemical compound or mechanical
mixture, whether included in the foregoing classes or not, which from its great susceptibility to detonation is suitable for employment in percussion capsoT>any other appliances for developing detonation or which, from its extreme sensibility to explosion, and from its instability is especially dangerous.
(f) CLASS, VI.-Ammunition-meaning an explosive of any of 'the foregoing
classes when enclosed in any case or contrivance, or otherwise' adapted' or prepared so as to form a cartridge charge for small' arms, caimon otother weapon, or for blasting, or to form any fuses fot blasting, or for shells, or to form any tube for firing explosives, or to form a percussion cap, detonator, fog-signal, shell, torpedo,or war rocket, or other contrivance other than a firework.
(g) CLASS VII.-Fireworks-meaning Firework composition and manufactured'
fireworks.
(2) vVhen an explosive falls into the description of more than one class it shall be deemed to belong exclusively to the latest class within the description of 'which it falls.
8. The packing of explosive and other dangerous goods intended for carriage by Railway, and the quantities in each package shall be as set out in Appendix :\0. 1 to these Bye-laws. ,
9. Explosives shaH be packed and labelled as required by these Bye-laws, and must be so addressed as to enable speedy delivery to be made.
10. No explosive shall be sent to the Railway for conveyance unless the depart-ment has been given 24 hours previous notice in writing of the intention; unless the consignee is prepared to at once take delivery of it; and unless the sender has rf.ceived notification that the Railway is prepared 'to receive such consignment.
11. Such notice shall contain the true name or mimes and description or descrip-tions, and the quantity of such explosives proposed to be conveyed, together with the name of the sender and consigne~ with their addresses.
12. Such consignments shall be received only during the hours of daylight, and at or between such times, as the department may require, and by the persons only duly appointed tQ receive and deliver such explosives and dangerous goods.
13. Every person employed in the loading or unloading of Explosives shall take all due precaution, in the receiving, loading, packing in the vans, breaking out or 'delivering of such explosives, to prevent such explosives being brought in.tocontact with, or endangered by any other article or substance liable to cause fire or explosion.
14. In handling, the package shall as far as possible be passed from hand 'to hand without shock or impact, and shall not be rolled on the ground, allowed to fall, or be set down roughly, and in no case shall such a package be rolled on the ground unless clean hides, cloths, or -Larp'aulins have been first carefully laid on the gr~l1nd .over which it is intended the explosive shall be passed, and aU packages of explosive sllall be packed'into the vans in a manner to prevent their displacement during transit.
15. ~o person while engaged loadingordischarging explosives, or approachilig the place where such work p.ither is, or has been lately, in progress shall wear b()ots or shoes with steel or iron tips, heels, nails, or protection, or other metal ",ork, or have about his person any matches or explosives, or means of striking '3, light, a'nd all persons so employed in loading or unloading or storing explosives, or who shall be,in proximity to the place where explosives are being, or have been worked recently, , shall refrain from smoking. ' .
16. When the handling bf explosives is going on all persons engaged shall observe all precautions for the prevention of accidents by fire 'Or explosion, and for preventing unauthorised persons having access to the explosives, and to prevent any other person committing any such act, and any such person who commits any such act,' and any stranger who after being warned commits any such act, shall be deemed to have com-mitted an offence against these Bye-laws.
17. The loading, unloading, or storage of explosive once begun shall be continued with all reasqnable speed to completion.
18. Packages containing'explosive shalll:ie removed by'the consignee in accordance with his undertaking to the Railway, from its premises as soon as possible, iJ,nd 'With all due diligence, and if not removed within 6 hours may be sold forthwith; or other-wise disposed of as the department thinks fit, and the department shall haye no liability to the o'wners or consignees in respect of such explosi\'e, beyond the sum obtained for it on sale, after dedllcting therefrom any costs which the Railway may have incurred in connection with it., 19. Packages of explosives shall not be put into any shed of the department, and shall at all times be kept covered with painted cloth, tarpaulin or other suitable nlaterial.
20. A gunpowder van shall not be loaded ,yith more than 4 tons 9 C"lvt. 1 qr. 4Ib., or 10,000 lb. of Explosive.
21. Charges in all cases shall provide for conveyance at mvner's risk.

The general rules referred to are:-
( The expression" outer package" means a box, barrel, case or cylinder of
wood, metal, or other solid material of such strength, construction and character that it shall not be broken or accidentally opened, nor become defec-· tive or insecure' whilst being conveyed, and will not allow any explosive to escape.
Inner package means a substantial case, bag or canister, or other receptacle,.
made and closed so as to prevent any explosive from escaping.
2. The interior of every package shall be free frorri grit and otherwise c1ean_ 3. Save as above provided there shall not be any iron or steel iIi the con-
struction of any package unless the same be covered with suitable material so as to effectually prevent the exp~sure of such iron or steel.
4. Every package when actually used for the packing of an explosive shall
not be used for the packing of any other explosive, or of any other article or-substance.· . .
Provided that the packing of inner packages containing a propellant, .in.
another package with inner packages containing gunpowder or 'Other pro-pellant, is not prohibited by this rule. . .
Provided also that this rule shall not prohibit the packing'of any arti«le·
which is not inflammable Or of an explosive nature, or liable to cause explosion in the same package as explosives of the ammunition .class, q.s eilUmerated.
5. Nothing in these rules shall be deemed to prohibit the use of an additional
package provided that such additional package shall not b.e of such a chara.cter·, as shall have been prohibited. .
6. A.n explosive which is not an authorised explosive, shall be packed in such.
manner as may be directed.
7. On the outermost package there shall be affixed in conspicuous characters·
by means of a brand or securely attached mark the word" Explosive ", the. name of the explosive, the numper of the class to which it belongs, and the name of the maker, and of the sender.
In the case of Classes III and IV there shall be added the elate of manu-
facture.
In the case of ammunition which does not contain its own means of ignition,.
the marking shall be as for this class of explosive, when not so made up.
In the case of safety cartridges, percussion caps, and Railway fog signals,.
the words may be added" Not liable to explode in bulk." .'
Pin fire cartridges to be so marked.
8. "Propellant" means an explosive of Class III ada pte d and intended.
exclusively for use as a propelling charge in cannon or small arms.
APPENDIX II.
. Explosives-Specification of Packages .
'Wooden boxes and cases unless made of approved three or more plywood, must have the top, sides and bottom at least 1/2 inch thick and the ends at least 1/2 inch thick when dove-tailed, or 'with glued locked corners (locked COIners not to exceed. 1/8 inch in breadth) or 1 inch thick when neither dove-tailed nor locked cornered. When made of three ply wood the boxes or cases must comply with specification given. \Vooden barrels and cylinders must have a minimum thickness of l/Z inch.
Metal cylinders and metal cases for conveyance of explosives:-
The body may be of wrought iron, mild steel, hard rolled brass IVluntz-metal or
zinc, rivetted or lap-welded, fitted at both ends 'with a substantial flange of same material, or of gun metal, for the attachment of the lid and bottom. If of iron or steel to be thoroughly galvanized after rnaking up-thickness not to be less than 18 B.W.G. or if of zinc not less than 3/8 inch except bottom which shall not be less than 1/8 inch or if of zinc not·less than 3/8 inch. The lid not to be less than 1/16 inch at centre and 1/8 inch at rim, or if of zinc not less than 3/8 inch throughout. The lid to be securable with not less than four good screws, bolts or swing bolts, with or' without a substantial hinge which may take the place of one of the bolts or screws,
A washer of leather, india-rubber, or other suitable material to be between the
lid and the flange unless the lid is fitted with a projecting ring fitted into a depression on the flange.
",\11 rivet heads to be well finished and the inside of packages to be free from rough
edges or burrs.
The whole to be of good material and workmanship and to be rnaintained 111
efficient condition.

GENERAL CONDITIONS.
In order to prevent accidents arising from the conveyance of Gunpowder or other explosive by the Railway as far as possible, the department declines to receive any such goods except under the Railway (Carriage of Explosives) Bye-laws.
No person shall send to the RailV'.'"ay any consignment of explosive unless he shall have previously given notice of his intention in writing, and that the consignee is prepared to receive it on arrival, having received notice that the Railway is prepared to receive it and that he sends it to the Railway at the time named by the Railway for its reception. The notice to the Railway must state the true name, description and quantity of the explosive proposed to be conveyed, the name and address of the sender and of the consignee and the stations from and to which the consignment is to be carried. In the case of Nitro Compounds and Chlorate Mixtures, the date of Manufacture must be added.
Consignments of explosive shall be sent to the stations andshall be receiv:ed only during the hours of daylight, as the Railway shall appoint, and immediately on. arrival there shall be delivered to and received by the Railway servants authorised to receive dangerous goods, and by no other person.
No gunpowder or other explosive will be received or conveyedunle.ss plainly . marked and fully addressed to the consignee, and unless on the outer package ,con-taining the same there shall be printed in conspicuous characters the descriptio?-of . the inner package, and the word "Explosive" followed b.y the name of the· explosive and the class and division to which it belongs together with the name' and address of the owner and sender, and further any explosive must be packed securely in :;. sufficient package, which cannot leak, and in good condition.
No explosive will be loaded, unloaded, or dealt with except during the hours.' of daylight and during such hours between sunrise and sunset as shall be appointed~
The consignor, consignee, their agents or servants shall in bringing to, or hiking.·· from the Railway premises, exercise every care, and fulfil every requirement to, secure safety, and shall observe the requirements of the Bye~laws. , .
In loading or unloading any explosive the packages shall as far as possible' be passed from hand to hand, shall not be rolled upon the ground unless properly' prepared, shall not be thrown or dropped down, and must be handled from shock 01' impact, and shall not be brought into contact with, or be so placed as to be-, endangered by anything likely to cause fire or explosion.
No person engaged in bringing to or taking explosive from the Railway, shall have upon him any matches, or means of striking a light, nor shall any fire or light'be brought near to any explosive, and smoking is strictly prohibited. No person so, engaged shall 'wear boots with iron, steel or metal protection or nails thereon.
The loading or unloading of explosive when once begun shall be continued without interruption until completed.
Packages containing explosive must be removed from the Railway premises by the consignee as soon as practicable, and with all due diligence after arrival, and if not removed within six hours, the consignment may be sold or otherwise dealt with as the department thinks fit. .
The penalty for breach of any of the Railway (Carriage of Explosives) Bye-laws. is $96.
If the goods be not accepted in due course by the consignee they will at the option of the Railway be forthwith sold by the Railway, or returned to the sender, who will if they are returned be bound to receive them at once and pay all the charges incurred.

In consideration of the Railway consenting to carry the above-mentioned goods as above requested, I, the undersigned sender, do hereby undertake and agree as follows:-
(1) That the Bye-laws for the carriage of inflammable liquids, and the
Regulations an.d conditions printed on the back hereof have been in all respects complied with in regard to the above-mentioned goods.
(2) That I will indemnify the Railway against all claims and injury to persons
or property arising from non-compliance with the before-mentioned Regulations and conditions as to such goods, and will pay full compensation for all injury to its servants and damage to its property so arising, unless it can be proved that the injury or damage is due to the wilful neglect of Railway servants.
(3) That the said goods are to be carried at my sale risk, and that the Railway
is not under any circumstances to be liable for loss or injury or to delay to the said goods, except upon proof that such loss or injury was due to wilful misconduct on the part of the Railway servants.
(4-) That the goods are to be removed by the consignee from the station to
whic~ they are consigned within four working hours (except in the case of tank wagons, on which the time allowance is six hours) after delivery to the con-signee of an advice note of the arrival of the goods at such station, and that
43 (2)
in default of such removal, and so long as the goods or any part thereof remain on the premises of the Railway, the department shall be entiLkd to char"'e $.1.20 per ton per hour, or minimum $1.20 per consignment-as and f~r liquidated damages, for so long a time as the goods remain on the premises and further if such goods are n.ot removed within six working hours after delivery of the advice note, the Railway may sell the goods in such manner . and at such time as it thinks best, and retain out of the proceeds, all the charges payable for carriage, and all charges and expenses incidental to such sale, and also all other monies payable to it under this agreement, and I also agree to the conditions on the back hereof.
In witness whereof I have hereunto set my hand the day and year first above written.

GENERAL CONDITIONS.
1. Each package, also tank wagon before delivery to the Railway must have attached a label printed in conspicuous character, bearing the words "highly inflammable" and stating the precise nature and name of contents together with names and addresses of sender and consignee.
2. Consignments vvill not be received on a Friday for the Arima-Sangre Grande Line, or any Saturday, the days preceding, or on any Public holidays or days prior to, or 011 which goods service are suspended by public notice for any lines.
3. Carriage must in every case be prepared on the weights ascertained at the H.ailway Station.
4. The traffic y,'ill not be stored in any of the Raihvay sheds or enclosures. The' General Manager of Railways may,' in his absolute discretion, permit of small quantities of Bisulphide of Carbon and ant destroyer containing Bisulphide of Carbon being carried in strong stoppered bottles tied over, and luted, securely packed in sawdust, or mixture of sawdust and chalk, in strong wooden cases. Each .bottle must be separated from the other bottle by a wooden partition and the total weight of Bisulphide of Carbon in each case not to exceed 30 lb.
The case to be securely fitted so that the packing cannot escape, and to bear outside in bold letters the nature of the contents and the ,vords " HIGHLY INFI,AMMABLE " 1.l0t to be sent by any train arriving at its destination after 4-.00 p.ni. j not to be stored in any of the Railway buildings; and not to be accepted on any condition if :showing scent or sign of leakage.
5. Loading and discharging must .be cornpleted during daylight· and when ,commenced must proceed continuously. . ' .
6. Only tank wagons constructed in accordlince with: the specificationprovidecl by the English Railway Clearing House for tank wagons intended tocari'y inflammable liquids must be used for their conveyance.
7. Liquids carried in tank wagons and approved vessels must 'comply- ,i-ith the pressure tests prescribed.
8. All tank wagons must have been inspected and certified to be in good condition aTJd free from all leakage. .
9. Tank wagons must not be filled or emptied within 20 yards of any fire, flan'le, 'furnace, nor at any place where it may be exposed to sparks.
10. In filling tank wagons, iron casks or drums, an air space of at least 10.0 per cent. of the total capacity of the tank must be left. Tank wagons must in no case be loaded beyond their specified weight carrying capacity.
11. The lids and all inlets and outlets of tanks and vessels must be tightly and properly secured-free from leakage. . .
12. Returned empties will not be accepted for conveyance unless securely bunged or otherwise closed airtight. '
13. The transit shall in no case extend beyond the time stated for delivery under the Bye-laws, i.e., the expiration of four hours or six hours as the case may be after notice of arrival of the goods is given to the consignee personally or delivered at his address.
14. After the termination of the transit, goods carried or conveyed by the Railway will be subject in addition to the charge paid for carriage, to a further charge of $1.20 per ton per hour, minimum $1.20 per consignment, until they are removed from the Railway premises provided this charge shall not be made if the Railway has not given proper opportunity for the removal of the goods, and a similar charge will be made with respect to goods, the delivery of which cannot be effected by the Eailway in consequence of incorrect or insufficient address.
15. Senders ordering tanks and failing to load them and order them away in time
for the succeeding goods trains, will be subject to charges of $2.40 per day for each day they shall be detained.
16. The Railway will not be liable for any loss of mark.et profit, indirect or consequential damage in respect of goods lost, injured or delayed.
17. No claim in respect of goods for loss or damage during transit, for which it may be alleged the Railway is responsible, will be admitted unless the same is made in writing within three days after delivery of the goods in respect of which the claim is rn.ade, such delivery to be considered. complete at the termination of transit as already provided.
18. The. Railway will· not be responsible for goods improperly or insufficiently packed or addressed either for loss, damage, leakage or otherwise.
19. The Railway will not be responsible for goods consigned to destination beyc)l1d its lines nor-in any case unless attention is called to any neglect which is relied upon as the base of any claim before the goods leave the Eailway premises.
Consigll1llent Note for the Carriage of Bisulphide of Carbon, and
~nt Destroyer containing Bisulphide of Carbon.
The Railway gives notice that it will not carry Bisulphide of carbon unless the' following conditions are strictly complied with, and the contract hereinafter appearing. is signed by the person or Firm on whose behalf the Bisulphide of carbon is sent.
CONDITIONS.
Steel or 'wrought iron drums or barrels used for Bisulphide of carbon must comply ,'lith the specification set out in the Appendix to the Bye-laws. .
Each drum or barrel must be securely packed in a strong wooden case (which must be perforated), or ill a strong crate or cradle capo.ble of and calculated to protect it from injury.
VVhen filling the drums or barrels an air space of at least 10 per cent. must be left. Each drum or barrel when filled, before being handed to the Railway must be tested by the sender, and the absence of evaporation or leakage must be proved.
Each package before delivery to. the Railway must have a label attached, on which must be printed in conspicuous characters the words" Bisulphide of carbon-highly inflammable" and the names and addresses of the sender and the consignee.
The screw bung of eadi dr\lm or barrel must be securely fastened in its place to prevent the escape of vapour, before being handed to the Railway for return.
Loading and unloading will be performed only during daylight-consignments will not bereceiv.ed on any Public holiday, on the days preceding such ho.lidays, on any Friday for the Arima-Sangre Grande Line, or on any Saturday for any lirie.
The carriage must be prepaid in every case. This traffic will not be stored in any of the Railway sheds or enclosures. In consideration of the Railway Department consenting to carry the Bisulphide of carbon which may be delivered to it on my behalf, I hereby undertake and agree:- .
That the above conditions shall be in all respects complied with in regard to
all such Bisulphide of carbon. . .
That the Railway shall not be liable for any loss of, or injury to such Bisulphide
of carbon, unless such loss or injury is proved to have been caused by the wilful misconduct of its servants.
That I will indemnify the Railway against all claims for injury to persons or

That the goods are to be removed by the consignee from the station to which

GENERAL CONDITIONS.
1. The Railway Department gives notice that it is not a common carrier of inflammable liquids or dangerous goods..' - .
2. No claim in respect of goods for, loss or damage in transit for which it is alleged the Railway may be liable, will be allowed unless the same is made in writing within three days of the:delivery of the goods, and such delivery will be considered complete at the termination of transit as directed in the RailwayTolls, Rules and Regulations.
3. The transit shall in no case extend beyond-(a) the time when the goods ·conveyed are unloaded and tendered to the consignee, or to the address to which they are consigned (b) the expiration of 24 hours after notice shall have been sent 'consignee 6f their arrival, or posted to him at the address given, or given to him personally. After the completion of transit as described, the Railway ',:ill hold the goods as warehousemen only subject to the usual charges.
4. After the completion of transit goods carried or conveyed by the Railway will !be subject in addition to the charge for carriage, to a further charge for demurrage of $2.40 per truck per day, or in case of such goods as shall have been discharged to reasonable charges for .rent or services performed, until they are removed from the Railway premises and similar charges will be made with respect to goods which cannot be delivered by the Railway in consequence of incorrect or inslifficient address. Provided that no such charges shall be made if the Railway has not given proper opportunity for the removal of the goods.
5. Consignors ordering trucks and failing to load them up and order them. away lJY the follovving goods trains, will be subject to the usual charges for demurrage for every day or fraction of a day while each truck is detained.
6. All goods delivered to the Railway will be received and held, subject to a lien for money due to the Railway Administration from the owners of such goods, upon any account, and in case any such lien is not satisfied within a reasonable time from the date upon which the Railway first gave notice to the owners of goods of ,the t~xercise of the same, the goods may be sold by the Railway by a1,lction or otherwise, and the proceeds of such sale applied, to the satisfaction of every such lien and expense.' '
7. The Railway will in no case be liable for any loss of market, loss of sale or profit, indirect or consequential damages, ho'wever arising, nor for loss or damage to the goods resulting from their being improperly or insufficiently packed or protected.
8. The Railway will not be responsible for loss, delay, or damage or misdelivery of goods arising from improper or insufficient address. '
9. In any case when the Railway charges are not prepaid, the goods it is llnderstood are accepted only upon the condition that the sender remains liable for the payment of all amounts due the Raiiway in respect of them, withouiprejudice to the Railways right if any against the consignee or other person.
10. In respect of traffic of every description which loses weight in transit, through drainage, evaporation or any cause beyond the Railways control, carriage shall be paid on the weight as ascertained at the forwarding station.
LE,,-EL CROSSIXGS.
Regulations Il1ade under the Railways Ordinance with respect to
level crossings.
1. Any person 'who is desirous of exercising the rights conferred by section 28 of the Railways Ordinance (in these regulations rE;ferred to as the "adjoining landowner") shall give notice in 'writing of such desire to the General Manager of Railways.
2. The adjoining landowner shall together with such notice submit for the consideration of the General Manager a plan showing the position on the railway of the crossing which he is desirous of having constructed and also of the approaches. on either side thereof and such other and further information as the General Manager may require for the purpose of giving proper consideration to the subject.
3. It shall be in the discretion of the General Manager to decide whether a level crossing as shown in such plan shou14 be made at the place proposed, and he shall see that in the location of the crossing due regard is paid to the safety of the Railway and of the passengers and traffic along the same. ·4. The approaches to the crossing shall be so made as to ensure the safety of the Railway and of the passengers and traffic along the same, and before allowing a crossing to be n1.ade the General Manager shall be satisfied that the approaches, are of the nature above required.
5. If the General Manager decides that the crossing applied for may be made, he shall inform the adjoining landO'wner of his decision and shall send to him a detailed estimate of the cost of and incidental to the making of the crossing, together with a request that the amount of such cost shall bepaicl by the adjoining landowner to the General Manager within a specified time.
6. Upon such amount being paid the General Manager shall instruct the Engineer to lay down the crossing and such ,York shall be completed within a Teasonable time.
7. For safety, and for the working of any sUGh crossing, there shall be provided such and so many signals, indicators, apparatus, Tods, wires, connections and other fittings and appuTtenances to the satisfaction of the General l'ITanager, ,vhether on lands of the adjoilling owneT or of the Railway, as sha~l in his opinion be necessary or expedient. . , '
8. The crossing and approaches thereto, and all signals,apparatus, connections or material shall be at all times maintained by the adjoining landowner and may be altered, added to or varied by him from time to time to the satisfaction of the General Manager, but no alteratiol1,addition or variation shall be s.anctioned by the adjoining landowner without the consent of the General Manager in writing Erst had and obtained. '
9. The adjoining landowner and his servants shall at all times follow and obey the directions, whether general for the working of the J:\.ailway so far as they apply, or particular for the working of the crossing, of the General Manager. and Officers, of the Railvtay, in the workiIig of the crossing, and concerning the safety of all persons, of the line and works, and of the trains, minerals, goods, animals, com-modities or things in·or upon any part of the Railway.,
10. Sufficient gates 'with locks and all fittings which may be necessary or expedient" shall be provided at the points where the railway or tramway authorised under these Regulations crosses the railway fences, and the same shall be efficiently maintained by the adjoiIiing landowner to the satisfaction of the General Manager.
11. All necessary labour for watching and for attendance upon the crossing shall be provided by the General Manager and be under'his control.
12. All costs of whatever kind incuned by the General Jlilanager in connection 'with the maintenance or working of any crossing shall be deemed a cost of the crossing and shall be paid to the General Manager on accounts presented by him to the adjoining landowner from time to time and may be reco,'erecl as a debt from such landowner in any Court of competent jurisdiction.
13. If at any time in the opinion of the General Manager of Railways a crossing Jaid down in accordance with these regulations becomes dangerous or interferes with the economical working of the Railway, the General Manager may, at the cost of the Government, remove such crossing and may, at the cost of the Government, lay a new crossing at such place as in his opinion Vlrill be suitable.
14. ,Vherever the railway shall cross any public road on the level the gates erected and maintained at such level crossing under the allthority of section 10 of the Railways Ordinance shall be kept closed across the railway instead of across such road except ,vhen engines or carriages passing along the railway shall have occasipn to cross. such road.
Rules made under section 32 of the Railways Ordinance.
PART 1.
1. These rules may be cited as the Railways (Motor Services) Rules. 2. Passenger vehicles shall be run at such times as may be deemed necessary by
the General Manager of Railways.
PASSENGER TRAFFIC.
3. (1) The tares to be paid by passengers on the following routes shall be those. fixed from time to time by the General Manager of Railways but shall not be in excess of 3 cents per mile:-
Between Arima and Blanchisseuse.
Do. Mayaro and Guayaguayare. Do. Moruga and La Lune. Do. Taco and Matelot. Do. Rio Claro and Ecclesville.
Any rouie in the Island "Ward of Tobago.
(2) The fares to be paid by passengers on all other routes shall be those fixed from time to tirhe by the General Manager of Railways but shall not be in excess of .2 cents per mile.
(3) Children under 3 years of age in charge of a guardian and not occupying separate seats will be carried free. ' .
(4) Two-thirds of the passenger rates ordinarily paid by adults will be charged on all journeys for children over the age of three years and under the age of twelve years as well as for bona fide pupils and students over the age of twelve years on journeys to and from school.
4. (1) Weekly tickets available only for the perIod .Monday to Saturday (both days inclusive) will be issued for any points on any specified route at the rate of 12 cents per mile, with the exception of the routes set out in paragraph (1) of rule 3' of these Rules in respect of which a rate not exceeding 18 cents per mile may be charged. Such tickets shall not be transferable and shall not be deemed to entitle the holder of any such ticket to travel by any particular vehicle or at any particular time.
(2) Monthly tickets available for one calendar month terminating on the last day of the month will be issued for any points on any specified route at the rate of 52 cents per mile. .
Each ticket shall be valid for the calendar month for which it is issued, and for sixty journeys between the points specified thereon, or between either of those points and any intermediate point or between arty two intermediate points. Such ticket shall not be transferable and shall not be deemed to entitle the holder of any such ticket tottavel by any particular vehicle or at any particular time.
(3) The miniml].m charge for ordinary or weekly tickets Vlrill be computed as for a distance of 2 miles.

(1) Subject to the payment of a minimum' charge 'of $2.00 motor:{)ni.nl1:iuses may be chartered at the rates computed for the full seating capacity of the vehicle in accordance with the fares set out in rule 3, no. allowance being ma9.e for children,' and for a distance equal to the total distance travelled by .the vehicle from its starting ,point to its'destination and backtQ the said starting point. .

7. The'G!,neral Manager of Railways. reserves the right to . refuse to '" accept for' conveyance any consignment which is not properly secured, labelled or addressed.
8. (1) ·Whenever furniture or any fragile article is required to be conveyed, the· consignor or sender shall prior to its acceptance by the Railway Authorities fill up an Owner's Risk Consignment note in the following terms;-
The Railway Department is requested to receive and forward as per address
and particulars on this note the undermentioned furniture and packages to be carried at the sole risk of the owner, and upon the condition that the Railway is relieved from all liability for loss, damage, or misdelivery except upon proof that such loss, damage, or misdelivery arose from the wilful misconduct on the part of the Railway servants, to which I agree and I also agree that paragraphs 2, 3, 6, 7, 8 and 9 of the General Conditions set out on the back of Consignment Notes relating to the carriage of (1) inflammable liquids; and (2) dangerous or corrosive or poisonous chemicals shall mutatis mutandis apply to this con-signment.
