PART III.
PASSENGER AND GENERAL TRAFFIC OF THE MOTOR SERVICES O}<' THE TRINIDAD
GOVERl'/MENT RAILWAYS.
9. Each passenger shall upon demand pay to the conductor or other duly autho-rised servant of the Railway the fare legally payable for the journey. No person shall travel on an omnibus without a ticket showing the places between which, and the distance over which, he is being conveyed. Each passenger shall show and deliver up his ticket (whether a contract, season or other ticket) to any duly authorised servant of the Railway whenever required so to do by any such servant.
10. (1) No person shall use or attempt to use a ticket on any day or for any journey for which such ticket is not available, or use or attempt to use a ticket which,has been already used on a previous journey.
(2) No person shall aid or abet any passenger in the contravention of this rule.
11. No person shall use or attempt to use a pass or ticket for a longer distance than that for which it is available. .
12. No passenger shall vrilfully alter or deface a ticket so as to render illegible the date, number, or any 1Il.aterial portion thereof.
13. No person shall sell or attempt to sell or part or attempt to part with the possession of his ticket in order to enable any other person to travel therewith, and no person shall purchase such ticket or travel or attempt to travel therewith. .
14. No person shall smoke on an omnibus except on the t\vo rear seats. 15. No person who in the opinion of the conductor is in a state of intoxication shall enter, or attempt to enter any motor omnibus after being refused admission there-l~tO.' No person shall use profane, obscene or offensive language or in any way dIsturb the peace or interfere with the comforts of any person while in or upon any motor omnibus, lorry or other property of the Railway, or spit or commit any nuisance in, upon or against any motor omnibus, lorry or other such property. Every such person may irrespective of any proceedings which may be taken be removed from the omnibus, lorry or raihvay premises and in that event shall forfeit the fare if any which he ~as paid.
16. No person shall wilfully cut, tear, soil or damage any lining, window strap, cushion, curtain,. or any part of an omnibus or lorry, or remove or deface any number plate or notice, or break or scratch any window of any omnibus or lorry, or otherwise wilfully damage, deface or injure any other property of the Railway: and any such person may, irrespective of any penalty which may be imposed, be ~iable to pay the amount of any damage done.
17. No person shall travel on the hood, footboard or any portion of an omnibus or a lorry not intended for the conveyance of passengers, and any person persisting in doing so after being warned to, desist by any duly authorised servant of the Railway may irrespective of any proceedings which may be instituted be removed 'from the omnibus or lorry.
18. No person shall without the consent ofa duly authorised servant of the Railway enter an omnibus by the windows or emergency door, or open or attempt to open an emergency door of an omnibus.
19. No person shall mount, enter or leave or attempt to mount enter or leave an omnibus or a lorry while the vehicle is in motion.
20. No person shall attempt to enter or mount, or shall enter, mount or remain in an omnibus or a' lorry containing the full number of persons which it is con-structed to convey after a duly authorised servant of the Railway hq.s warned him against entering, mounting or remaining in the same .. A warning shall be given by the conductor on the application of any passenger.
21. No dog or other animal will be allowed to accompany passengers on an omnibus, but will be conveyed separately, and charged for in omnibuses in which there is provision for carrying freight. No person shall take a dog or other animal with' him into an omnibus in which no provision exists for the carrying of ~reight. 'Any dog or other animal taken into any motor, omnibus in breach hereof shall be immediately. removed by the 'person in charge of such dogor other animal upon being requested so to do and in default of compliance with such request it may thereupon be removed by the conductor from such omnibus. '
22. No person shall take any loaded firearms into an omnibus or a lorry. 23. (1) The Railway may refuse to convey any person suffering from any infectious disease.
(2) No person who has any dangerous infectious disease shall travel or attempt to travel on an omnibus, and no such person shall travel or attempt to .travel on a lorry without the special permission of the General Manager of Railways. '
(3) Any person who aids and abets any person suffering from a dangerous infectious disease in travelling or attempting to travel on an omnibus or in travelling or attempting to travel on any lorry without the special permission of the General Manager of Railways, shall be guilty of an offence against these rules. ,
(4) Every such person contravening this rule shall in addition to any penalty that may be imposed and in addition to the forfeiture of any fare which he may have paid be removed at the first opportunity from the omnibus or lorry. '
(5) In this rule "dangerous infectious disease" and "infectious disease" shall bear the same meanings as they respectively bear or may bear under the Public Health Ordinance.
24. Any person whose dress or person might in the opinion of the conductor soil -the dress or clothing of any passenger or who in the opinion of the conductor might for any reason be offensive to passengers shall not be entitled to be conveyed on any motor omnibus; but if he has boarded the omnibus shall leave the same after having been requested so to do by the conductor and in default of compliance with such request maybe removed therefrom and his fare, if previously paid, shall be returned to him. '
25. No person shall mount, enter or board an omnibus or lorry except flJr the :purpose of travelling,
26. No person shall carry or send or attempt to carry or send by any omnibus any ;gunpowder, lucifer or congreve matches, vitriol, ardent spirits, paraffin, petroleum, .kerosene, or other mineral oil, turpentine, naphtha, camphine or any article which in the judgment of the Railway authorities may be of an explosive or highly com-bustible nature, or shall send or attempt to send any of the said articles by Lony Service without distinctly marking the nature of such articles on the case containing them or otherwise giving notice in writing to that effect to the General IVIanager of Railways or the authorised person to whom they are delivered; and any person who ,shall wilfully fail to give a true account of the contents of any parcel or package conveyed by an omnibus or a lorry, or who shall wilfully give a false account of the same to any servant of the Railway Department receiving or conveying such parcel ,or package, shall be guilty of an offence against these rules.
27. No person shall wilfully obstruct, imp·ede or assault any officet or servant of ·the Railway in the execution of his duty.
28. Any person contravening, or committing a breach of any of these rules shall be guilty of an offence and shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine of forty-eight .dollars; .
29. Small parcels of personal luggage which can be carried inside an omnibus without inconveniencing other passengers will be carried free of charge. Other ,.articles of personal luggage which cannot be conveniently' taken into the portion of ·the omnibus set aside for passengers will be carried in the freight compartment where .an omnibus is licensed as a freight passenger vehicle.
" Personal luggage " is understood to:mean such articles of necessity or convenience .as are usually canied by passengers for their personal use and includes implements . .and tools ,of employment and groceries.
30. Ground provisions, fresh' fruit of all description, fish, paddy rice, agricultural produce, ice, parcels or books, newspapers, mineral water in boxes, poultry in crates, milk in crates, will be carried only in the freight compartment provided for that purpose.
31. The following charges will be made for 2JI articles carried in the freight ,compartment :-
Packages not exceeding 25 lb. in \-veight ... 12 cents for any distance
Packages over 25 lb. in weight but not
exceeding 50 lb. 24
Packages over 50 lb. in weight but not
exceeding 75 lb. 36
Packages over :75 lb. in weight but not
exceeding 100 lb. . 54
~ 0 package weighing more than 100 lb. ,vill be accepted for conveyance by any -Dmnibus. .
Breakfast baskets will be carried at the rate of $2.00 per month for any distance.
PART IV.
RULES RELATING TO DRIVERS OF lVloTOR VEHICLES.
Every driver of a Railway omnibus or lorry shall comply with the rul~s hereinafter' :set forth, namely~
32. He shall wear his badge conspicuously and in such a manner that the whole of the writing thereon is distinctly legible.
33. He shall be suitably attired in uniform. 34. He shall collect the ticket of every passenger immediately before the passeno-er
alights from the omnibus and shall place such ticket in the container provided for that purpose.
35. He shall report to the clerk at the Office the number of passengers, if any, -who
have not delivered up any such ticket.
36. He shall not convey on his own account any packages for or on behalf of any
person.
37. He shall not smoke while driving any such vehicle. 38. He shall not make use of any obscene, abusive or offensive language or insulting gestures.
39. He shall not be under the influence of drink or drugs while driving any such vehicle. ~
40. He shall carefully check the gas, oil, water and tyre p~essures on his vehicle-and be satisfied that everything is in order before leaving the Garage. He will be held responsible in case of any troubles due to neglect of the above.
41. He shall promptly obey the signal of any Inspectors of this Department. 42. He shall on his arrival at the outer Terminal hand over all collected tickets to the Ancillary Clerk stationed there for cancellation.
43. He shall immediately upon his return to the Head Despatching Office, or Railway Station together with the Conductor check with and hand over to the Ancillary Clerk stationed there, all tickets and cash collected on each individuaL trip. -The driver and _the conductor of each vehicle shall both be present when the tickets and cash are being checked and handed over to the Ancillary Clerk.
44. He shall enter each and every. trip in the Vehicle ~ BOElk, together with the mileage, of each individual trip. - •. - -
45. He shall enter speedometer readings at commencement of duties and completion of duties in the Vehicle Log Book, provided for each vehicle.
46. He shall enter gasolene and oil taken each and every' day in the V ehicle~ Log Book, provided for _each vehicle.
47. He shall .make a note of any incidents which occur during. pis. opera,tion'_of the vehicle, in the Vehicle LogBook. -
48. He shall fill in a Daily Bus or Truck Return-sheet as the case may be on completion of work on each vehicle, and hand in 'same to the Ancillary_ ·Clerk in_ charge, before going off duty each day. .
49 .. He shall keep a record in the Vehicle Log Book, 'of all tools supplied to that particular vehicle, when taking over the vehicle. Drivers will' oe -held ~esponsible-in case any of the said tools are missing, or brokeJl. -
50. He shall send a written report in case of any damage to the vehicle which he is driving or of the loss of any missing parts, or accessories.
51. He shall deliver to the responsible Ancillary Clerk each day on completion of his duties the ~witch key, the gas alene tank key and the tool box key.
52. He shall not use on another vehicle a Log Book supplied for one vehicle. 53. He shall enter his name and time of arrival and departure each day in-the " Drivers' and Conductors' -Attendance Book" provided therefor at the Ancill~ry Office.
Any driver who fails to comply with any of the provisions of these rules shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine of forty-~ight dollars ..
PART V.
RULES RELATING TO CONDUCTORS.
Every conductor of a Railway omnibus shall comply with the rules hereinafter set forth, namely-
54. He shall wear ,his badge conspicuously and in such a manner that the whole of the writing thereon is distinctly legible.
55. He shall be suitably attired in uniform. 56. He shall immediately after a passenger has boarded an omnibus demand the legal fare for the journey from the passenger and on payment of same shall deliver to such passenger a ticket to cover the entire journey, and shall instruct such passenger to deliver up the said ticket to the driver immediately before alighting from the omnibus.
57. He shall report to the clerk at the Ancillary Office any failure of the driver to run the omnibus according to schedule and shall state to such clerk the cause of any such failure.
58. He shall report to the said clerk any contravention by the driver of the NIotor Vehicles and Road Traffic Ordinance and the Regulations made thereunder.
59. He shall report to the said clerk any complaint made to him by any passenger or any incident affecting any passenger which may have occurred during the journey.
60. He shall issue from the book provided for that purpose receipts for all packages (other than small parcels of personal luggage which are allowed free of charge) brought by a passenger into the omnibus.
61. He shall not convey on his own account any package for or on behalf of any person.
62. He shall not smoke while on duty on an omnibus. 63. He shall keep all cash, receipt books, and tickets and other property of the Trinidad Government Railway in the bag supplied to him for that purpose and shall not mix his own money or property with that of the Railway.
64. He shall account to any officer appointed for that purpose at all times on which he may be called upon so to do and 'shall produce for the inspection of the' said Officer all money, tickets and receipts being the property of the Railway.
65. He shall enter his name and time of arrival and departure each day in the " Drivers' and Conductors' Attendance Book" provided therefor at the Ancillary Office.
66 .. He shall not make use of any obscene, abusive or offensive language or insulting, gestures:
67. He shall not be under the influence of drink or drugs while in charge of an omnibus.
68. He shall immediately upon his return to the Head Despatching Office or Railway Station together with the driver check with and hand over to the Ancillary Clerk stationed there all cash collected on each individual trip. The driver and conductor of each vehicle shall both be present when the tickets and cash are being checked and handed over to the Ancillary ClerIc
69. Any conductor who fails to comply with any of the provisions of these rules. shall be liabl~ on summary conviction to a nne of forty-eight dollars.
PART VI.
70. All employees of the motor services of the Railway shall be subject to the- General rules of the Rules and Regulations for the guidance of officers and servants in the service of the Trinidad Government Railway made by the Governor in Council on the 7th. February, 1924.
ChA.?1E~ 18. No. 1.
HARBOURS.
Regulations Ulade under Section 63 of the Harbours Ordinance.
GENERAL.
L Vessels having drogher certificates when under sailor steam within the waters of the Colony between the hours of sunrise and sunset shall carry at the masthead, or if vessels havingmor:e than one mast, at the foremasthead, a blue fiag with a white cross in the centre.
2. Vessels which have been boarded in any harbour of the Colony shall on arrival in another harbour of the Colony hoist their colours at the foremasthead.
In case of any contravention of this regulation the master or person in charge shall be liable to a penalty of $5.00. ' .
3. Every licensed boat shall have the number of its licence and the initial of the harbour where it is licensed and the name of the owner legibly painte.d on the stern in letters and figur~s not less than one and a half inches in length, and not on a board moveable at pleasure,
In C2"se of any contravention of this regulation the owner shall be liable to 8. penalty of $24.00.
4. All licensed boats shall be licensed to carry a'specified number of .passengers and no licensed boat shall carry more than the number so specified.
For the purpose of arriving at the number of passengers ,to be carried, the Harbour Master shall allow one 'passenger for everyone arid a half lineal feet of .seating accommodation in such boat, provided, however, that when such accommodation is of an unusual character he may allow such number of passengers as. heJ;tlay consider adequate. • .
III case of any contravention of this regulation every boatman in the .boat s113,11 be liable to a penalty of $24:00.
5. Droghers when at anchor at night shall exhibit a bright 'white light visible all round the horizon.'
6. Boats (licensed or not) when moving about in harbours at night shall exhibit a bright light carried at least two feet above the gunwale ali.d so fixed tha,t it can be seen all rQund the compa~s.
This regulation shall applyaiso to fishing boats, whether moving or stationary, when fishing within the limits of harbours, but shall not apply to Police or Custolns .boctts.
7. If any person shall by any act or default commit any breach of regulations 5 or 6, such person shall be liable to a penalty of $24,00 for each such breach, and' the drogher, boat 01" vessel in respect of which sllch breach was committed may be charged with the payment of such penalty.
HARBOUR OF PORT-OF-SPAIN.
8. Unless otherwise provided for in these regulations, no lighter, launch, 'boat or coasting vessel shall lie alongside the wharves betwcei1 the hours of 6 p.m. and 6 a.m. and on Sundays and Public Holidays, unless they are actually working at such times, but shall proceed to one of the places assigned to them hereunder: Provided, however, that vessels loaded at the wharves for the coast may remain alongside after 6 p.m. if they are to leave during the night immediately following such loading.
(a) Empty lighters, and water boats whether laden or not shall anchor
East of 2 black buoys in line with the eastern side of the Railway Export Shed on the Queen's Wharf.
(b) Vessels with cargo for or from the Railway Export Shed or Railway
Wharf may remain alongside such places; if for any reason they cannot remain alongside, they shall anchor between the shore and the two inner buoys off the Railway Export Shed and the Harbour Master's boathouse.
(c) Small craft from the West India Islands and Venezuela, coasting vessels,
launches and boats using the Queen's Wharf, shall anchor within the area, marked by 2 red buoys in line with the eastern side of the Harbour Master's Boathouse and 2 red buoys in line with the ridge of the Public Weighbridge Building on the Queen's Wharf.
(d) Lighters with cargo for the Custom House or with cargo from the Custom
House awaiting shipment to an exporting ship shall lie alongside the Customs wharf or Customs Jetties. If for any reason they cannot lie alongside, they shall anchor within the area marked by 2 white buoys in line with the eastern side of the Customs' bonded Warehouse and 2 white buoys in line with the eastern jetty of the Custom House. Lighters with lumber, coal, bricks, loose iron or machinery from an importing ship for discharge at the public wharves may remain alongside such wharves at all times until discharged.
(e) Lighters with cargo loaded at the wharves or with cargo from the coast
awaiting the arrival of an exporting ship shall anchor within the area marked by 2 red buoys in line with the western jetty of the Custom House and 2 red buoys in line with the eastern side of the Customs Office on St. Vincent Wharf. Lighters which have not completed loading for an exporting ship shall also anchor within this area from 6 p.m. to 6 a.m. and on Sundays and Public Holidays if not working at such times.
(f) Boats and launches using the St. Vincent Wharf, when not employed,
shall ancLor within the area marked by 2 white buoys off the lO-ton crane and parallel to St. Vincent Jetty and 2 white buoys off the foot of Richmond Street and also parallel to St. Vincent Jetty.
(g) No vessel shall anchor in any of the above described anchorages so that
she can swing between the wharves and the buoys nearest the wharves or into any of the spaces between the various anChorages which are reserved for navigation to and from the wharves.
9. Vessels which have discharged at the wharves or warehouses shall remove at once to one of the anchorages assigned to such vessels, provided however that when such vessels are required to take in cargo on the same day of discharge they may remain alongside in order to secure the berth, but in no other case.
10. No vessel shall anchor in the channel leading to the St. Vincent Jetty or so that, when she swings, she shall lie nearer than t cable from the line of buoys on the side of the channel nearer to her.
11. No stationary moorings shall be permitted in the harbour without the approval in writing of the Harbour Master.
12. No vessels shall be permitted to remain alongside any landing place except when actually taking in or landing passengers.
13. No wares, merchandise, or other articles or packages, except small parcels and passengers' personal baggage and packages for the Government Steamers, shall be landed on or taken off the Jetty at Queen's Wharf.
14. The penalty for any breach of the regulations numbered from 8 to 13 (both inclusive) shall be a fine of $24.00.
T,-VIII. 44
HARBOUR OF SAN FERNANDO.
15. All goods shall be landed on the wharf space between the north side of the
Government Petroleum Store and the south east end of the wharf, and the Harbour Master may appropriate particular portions of this space for the landing of particular classes of goods. Moreover, the Harbour Master. may give directions as to the time during which goods landed shall be allowed to remain when landed, and as to particular classes of goods may require that they be immediately removed to some adjacent ground. The Harbour Master may also give directions as to the time during which goods or anything deposited within 20 feet of the edge of the wharf shall be allowed to remain when so deposited.
In case of any contravention of this regulation or of any directions given by
the Harbour Master thereunder the offender shall be liable to a penalty of $24:00 and the goods or things in respect of which the contravention takes place shall be charged with the payment of the penalty. .
16. Boats shall be beached on such parts of the shore as the Harbour Master
may appoint for that purpose.
17. No boats shall be anchored within 50 yards of any part of the shore.
18. In case of any contravention of regulations 16 and 17 the offender shall be
liable to a penalty of $5.00 and the boat in respect of which the contravention takes place shall be charged with the payment of such penalty.
HARBOUR OF BRIGHTON (LA BREA).
19. All goods must be landed on the beach or' pier between Point d'Or and Point
Boyer, and the Harbour Master may appropriate particular portions of this space for the landing of particular classes of goods. Moreover the Harbour Master may give directions as to the time during which goods landed shall be allowed to remain . when lancl,ed, and as to particular classes' of goods may require that· they be . immediately after landing removed to some adjacent ground.
In case of any contravention of this regulation or of any directions given by the
Harbour Master thereunder the offender shall be liable to a penalty of $24.00 al1d the goods in respect of which the contravention takes place shall be charged with the payment of the penalty.
20. Boats may be beached .on any part of the shore .between Mr. McCarthy's
dwelling house and Point Boyer, but not .elsewhere except with the peqnission'.Q.f the Harbour Master. .
21. Fishing boats, corials and other open boats may be pulled up 'or anchored
to the west of .111r. McCarthy's dwelling house and to the east,of King's POint,but not elsewhere.
22. No boat shall be anchored within 50 yards of any part of the shore.
23. In case of any contravention of any of the three last pr-eceCling regulations
as to boats, the offender shall be liable to a penalty of $5.00, and the boat inrespect of which the contravention takes place shall be charged with the payment of such penalty.
FOR THE HARBOUR OF PORT-OF-SPAIN.
Regulations and Table of Boat Fares made under section 44 of the Harbours
Ordinance.
1. All boats shall lie off steamers and other vessels carrying passengers at a distance of not less than twenty yards except as hereinafter directed.
2. Not more than one boatshall be allowed to come alongside at the same time for the purpose of taking in or embarking passengers and their luggage.
3. No boat shall be permitted to remain alongside longer than is actually necessary to enable the boatman to take in or embark passengers and their luggage.
4. No boatman shall in any vessel lying within twenty yards or alongside of any steamer or other vessel carrying passengers-
(a) be drunk and disorderly; or
(b) make use of any ~iolent or obscene language with intent to provoke any
person to commit a breach of the peace; or
(e) use any obscene or profane language to the annoyance of any person; or
(d) fight or otherwise disturb the peace.
5. Any member of the Police Force, either from a patrol launch or from the· shore, may control all boats at or within 50 yards of any landing place or ship's gangway; and the coxswain .or any person in charge or forming part of the crew of any boat approaching, or leaving, or heaved to ,vithin 50 yards of such landing place or gangway shall obey all lawful orders given by a member of the Police Force, and shall at once move away from any landing place or from the gangway of any ship if so ordered.
6. Any boatman, porter or person exercising the calling of a porter who obstructs the traffic on the wharves or jetties and vessels alongside and does not move off on being directed to do so shall be liable to a penalty of $24.00.

or part of arnile for each passenger 24 Children under 12 years of age half the above rates. Baggage :-Each passenger is allowed one package and such small articles as
he carries in his liands, every additional package 06 For carriage of baggage only, without passengers, the fare for one passenger

above rates.

Regulations made under Section 63 of the Harbours Ordinance.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Port-of-Spain Harbours (Prohibition
of Bathing and Smoking) Regulations.
2. No person shall make use of the wharves or jetties mentioned in the Schedule hereto for the purpose of bathing in the waters adjacent thereto.
3. No person shall use any boat or other craft for the purpose of bathing in the waters adjacent to the said wharves or jetties.
4. No person shall smoke or carry any naked light on or within 100 feet of the Shell Leas~holds Distributing Jetty by the Archer Coaling Depot.
5. If any person shall contravene any of the preceding regulations he shall be
iable on summary conviction to a penalty of $24.00 and in default of payment to imprisonment for thirty days.
SCHEDULE.
That portion of the wharves extending from the Lighthouse Jetty to the
commencement of the Deep Water Wharf.
Lighthouse Jetty. St. Vincent Street Jetty. Shell Leaseholds Distributing Jetty by the Archer Coaling Depot.
Regulation m.ade under Section 63 of the Harbours Ordinance.
1. This regulation may be cited as the Protection of Beacons Regulation. 2. (1) No vessel shall be made fast to any public beacon in any Harbour.
(2) The master of any vessel contravening this regulation .~hall be liable' on summary conviction to a penalty of $24.00, and the vessel in respect of which such breach took place may be charged with the payment of such penalty.

(b) make use of any violent or obscene language with intent to provoke any
person to commit a breach of the peace; or
(e) use any obscene·or profane language to the annoyance of any person; Or

PILOTAGE.
Bye-laws :made by the Pilotage Authority under Sections 6 and 7 ,of
. the Pilotage Ordinance.
1. These Bye-laws may be cited as the Pilotage Bye-laws. 2. In these Bye-laws, unless the' context otherwise requires, the· following expressions have the meanings hereby assigned to them:-
" apprentice" means a person serving an apprenticeship to the Pilots'
Association, under the terms approved by the Authority, and who is in training as a candidate for a pilot's licence;
" the authority" means the Pilotage Authority established by section 4 of
the Ordinance;
" Basin " means the dredged portion of the harbour of Port-of-Spain abutting
on King's Wharf but does not include the dredged channel leading to such dredged portion;
" certificated officer" means a master or mate holding a pilotage certificate
granted to him by the Authority;
" committee" means a Committee of the Authority appointed by s,uch
Authority;
"compulsory pilotage areas" means the areas described in the Second
Schedule to the Ordinance;
" Grier. Channel" means the dredged charinel in Port-of-Spain harbour
leading to the Basin and extending in a north-easterly direction from a beacon in latitude 10° 38' 00" N.and longitude 61 ° 33' 65" W. and marked out by other beacons;
'" Harbour Master " means the Harbour:i'VIaster afPort-of-Spain:; .. ' " King's Wharf" means the deep water wharf built pn the. reclaimed aJ,"ea
south of Wrightson Road, Port-of-Spain, and extending from a point on i;he western prolongation of Marip.e Square and continuing in a south-westerly direction; . ,
" net registered tonnage" means the net tOnnage registered or ascertained
in accordance with the tonnage regulations afthe Merchant Shipping Act, 1894;
" optional pilotage areas" means the areas described in the First Schedule
to the Ordinance; . .
" Ordinance" means the Pilot~ge Ordinance; " out-port" means any of the compulsory pilotage area~ in the vicinitypf.
the Trinidad Leaseholds Jetty, the Pitch .Point Jetty, the Brighton Jettypr the Point Fortin Jetty; .' ...
"pilot" means a person holding a licence granted by.the Authority to'. pilot
vessels in one or more pilotage areas; '. .
"pilotage areas" include compulsory .pilotage areas and optional pilotage
areas;
".pilotage dues" means the payment to b~ made in respect of the servicECs
of a pilot;
"Pilots' Office" means the premises of the Pilots' Association at Port-of-
Spain;
" Secretary-Accountant" means the person .so appointed -by the Pilots'
Association with the approval of the Authority. 3. (1) A person shall not be licensed as a pilot unless and until he satisfies the Qualifica-
Authority that- . tions of
(a) he is a natural born British subject; for candidates Pilot's
(b) he is, at the time a licence is first granted him, more than twenty-five licence.
but not more than forty years of age;
(e) he holds a certificate as Master Mariner issued by the Government of
Trinidad and Tobago or an equivalent or higher certificate;
(d) he has served an apprenticeship of not less than six months with the
Pilots' Association under conditions approved by the Authority;
(e) he has produced certificates of good conduct and sobriety from employers
during-the three years immediately preceding his application for a licence;
(f) he has been passed. as medically fit by a medical practitioner approved
by the Authority;
(g) he has satisfied the Harbour Master and two first class pilots as to his
skill in handling ships, and his knowledge of navigation in each area for which he has applied for a licence;
(11) he has satisfied the examining committee constituted under these Bye-laws
that he has satisfactory knowledge of local conditions and regulations affecting the pilotage areas for which he has applied for a licence including-
(i) the courses and distances between any two places within the limits
of such pilotage areas;
(ii) the rise, velocity and set of the tides;
(iii) the depth and character of the soundings;
(iv) the best anchorages;
(v) the banks, shoals and other dangers;
(vi) the buoys and lights in such areas.
(2) Nothing in this bye-law shall invalidate a licence as pilot granted under the Pilots Ordinance, Cap. 120, and in force on December 27th, 1939, or preclude the renewal of such licence under the conditions under which it was originally issued, or preclude the extension of such licence for additional pilotage areas as may be approved by the Authority.
4. A pilot's licence shall remain in force for such period as the Authority may Duration of direct. . . licence.
The fees· prescribed in Schedule II to these Bye-laws· shall be payable to the Fees.
Authority on the issue or renewal of any licence prescribed in these J.3ye-laws.
5. (1) The number of pilots to be licensed for all areas shall be fourteen, but Temporary . this number may at any time be varied by resolution of the Authority if the pilotage licences, and requirements of shipping calling at the ports of the Colony so warrant. number of
pilots ..
(2) It shall be lawful for the Authority to grant temporary licences to pilot ships in any areas to one or more pilots in excess of the prescribed number whenever it is established to the satisfaction of the Authority that conditions necessitate such temporary a<;idition to the number of licensed pilots.
(3) A temporary licence granted under this Bye-law shall not be in force for a longer period than three months but maybe renewed from time to time for a like period, provided that a temporary licence shall not be renewed after the Authority considers that the conditions, which necessitated the issuance .of such a licence, no longer exist.
(4) A person holding a temporary licence shall be subject to all the provisions of these Bye-laws with the exception of sub-paragraphs (b) and (d) of paragraph (1) of Bye-law 3.
(5) Notwithstanding anything contained in these Bye-laws every person to whom a temporary licence is granted shall pay to the Authority a fee of $10.00 on the granting of the licence and a fee of $2.50 on each renewal of such licence.
6. Any pilot unable to perform his duties through sickness or other: cause shall Sickness to cause the Harbour Master to be notified immediately. be reported.
7. No pilot shall leave the Colony without the permission of the Chairman of the Authority; such permission shall be in writing and shall not be granted 'without the approval of the Authority.
8. (1) A pilot shall obey and execute all lawful orders and directions given and issued by the Authority, the Harbour Master, or the "\Vharf Superintendent. Orders affecting pilots who are members of the Pilots' Association shall be given through the Secretary, and when so given shall be deemed to have been given to the pilot or pilots concerned.
In a harbour or dock, a pilot shall also obey all lawful orders and directions of a Harbour or Dock Master relating to docking, undocking, towing or removing a ship under his charge, so long as such ship shall be within the limits of any such harbour or dock as the case may be.
(2) A pilot shall observe strict sobriety. He shall throughout the time he is in charge of a ship use his utmost care and diligence for her safety and the safety of other vessels or property. He shall, when necessary keep the lead going while the ship is under way.
(3) A pilot shall not lay a ship aground without a written order from the owner or master of such ship, or the agent of the owner.
(4) A pilot shall not leave a ship piloted by him until she is berthed alongside a wharf or jetty or brought to a safe anchorage or, if outward bound, until the appropriate limits referred to in Bye-law 9 have been reached.
(5) A pilot shall before leaving a ship piloted or attended by him hand a Pilotage Note in duplicate to the master, who shall enter thereon in ink all the particulars required, and sigh the Note when the services of the pilot are completed, retaining one copy for himself. The Note shall also be signed by the pilot.
(6) A pilot shall not bring a ship alongside any wharf or jetty, should the signal for the wharf or jetty be against him, or if no signal is usually used, unless he shall 'have nrst received permission from the person in charge of the wharf or jetty.
(7) A pilot shall behave with due civility towards the owner, master, and officers of any ship under his charge.
(8) A pilot about to take charge of a ship which is outward bound or which is about to be moved from the wharf or jetty where she is lying, shall go on board and report himself to the master or officer ill command before the appointed time so as to enable her to be moved out from the wharf or jetty or to proceed to sea. or to her destination.
(9) A pilot when on duty shall always have ,with him a reliable watch, official tide tables for the Colony, a copy of these Bye-laws and directions for the t~me being in force for the pilotage areas, and his licence.
(10) A pilot before leaving a wJ:1arf, or jetty or CJ,nchorage in ch~rge of a ship shall enquire of the master or officer in command whether the steering gear is connected and in proper working order, and when clear shall direct that the anchors be ready for letting to. A pilot boarding and taking charge of an inward bound ship shall direct that the anchors be ready for letting go without delay.
(11) A ,pilot shall not proceed to any port or place outside the pilotage area for which he is licensed for the purpose of piloting any ship into such area without the permission of the Harbour Master.
(12) A pilot shall not attend to give evidence at any trial or enquiry to which he is not a party (unless under subpoena) without permission of the Harbour Master, and a pilot subp6enal,d to give evidence shall at once report the' ~act in writing to the Harbour Master.
(13) A pilot who riotices within the pilotage areas for which he is licensed any alteration in any of the banks or channels, or any buoys or, beacons which have been driven away, broken down, damaged or displaced, or any circumstances affecting the safety of navigation, shall forthwith give a correct statement thereof in writing to the Authority.
(14) A pilot, when any accident has happened to, or been caused by, a ship while in his charge, shall as soon as possible report the facts in writing on the approved form to the Harbour Master.
(15) The duties of a pilot in regard to an outward bound ship shall commence as soon as such ship begins to unmoor for the purpose of proceeding to sea.
(16) On inward bound ships the duties of a pilot ~hall commence when he boards the ship in any position within a pilotage area.
(17) In the event of a pilot refusing to proceed to sea from any anchorage or 'from any wharf or jetty with any ship which he is qualified to pilot when requested to do so by the master, owner or agent of such ship. he shall at once give his reasons therefor in writing to the Authority if he is not a member of the Pilots' Association or, if he is a member of the said Association, to his Association which shall forward same to the Authority.
(18) If a pilot mislays his licence he shall forthwith give notice thereof to the Authority, stating the circumstances in which the licence was lost, and shall forthwith apply to the Authority for a duplicate licence.
(19) A pilot shall not hold directly or indirectly any share or interest in any tug company, or in any vessel used regularly or occasionally for towing ships in the pilotage areas.
(20) On landing at Port-of-Spain after piloting a ship in or' out, all pilots for all pilotage areas shall report their movements without delay to the Pilots' Office. ,At outports, pilots shall report to the said office by telephone on landing from a ship piloted in or out between the hours of 8 a.m. and 5 p.m. each day.
(21) Pilots for all pilotage areas are to be available at t:q.e shortest possible notice and shall be connected by telephone at their residence.
(22) All pilots shall attend frequently at the Pilots' Office to examine the latest charts of the Colony, notices to mariners and other information relating to the waters of the Colony.
(23) A pilot shall supply himself, at his own cost, with, and wear when on duty either ashore or afloat, such uniform (if any) as maybe required by the Authority.
9. The limits for pilots boarding or leaving ships shall coincide with the limits specified in bye-laws 13 and 14.
10. Pilots shall be allotted to ships in all pilotage areas by the Senior Pilot subject to any special directions of the Harbour Master or Wharf Superintendent.
11. No boat shall ply as a pilot boat or carry a pilot flag or pilot boat light or make signals prescribed under Article 8 of the Regulations for the Prevention of Collision at Sea in or near any area prescribed in the First and Second Schedules to the Ordinance unless a pilot is on board. '
Any boat engaged solely for the purpose of embarking or disembarking pilots in ships shall have the words" Pilot Boat" painted on the bow in plain letters.
12. Pilotage dues in respect of pilotage services rendered by licensed pilots in the several pilotage areas shall be calculated in accordance with the rates laid down in bye-laws 13 and 14 and in Schedule I to these Bye-laws. All these rates shall be subject to .such surcharge or rebate as may from time to time be fixed by the Authority and approved by the Governor in Council. *
13. (1) Pilotage dues in respect of services rendered by licensed pilots in compulsory pilotage areas shall be calculated as follows-
(a) the relevant charge prescribed in Schedule I to these Bye-laws shall be
made;
(b) an additional charge, based on the maximum draught of the ship, at
the time the pilotage services are rendered shall be made in respect of ships navigating the Grier Channel or Basin; such charge shall be twenty-five cents per foot with a minimum draught of ten feet;

Sixty per centum on all rates laid down in Bye-laws 13 (1) (a) (b) and 14 (1)
and (2).

(e) between the hours of 6 a.m. and 6 p.m. a charge of $7.00 shall be made
in respect of running lines when such operation is carried out with the aid of a pilot's launch or launches.
Between the hours of 6 p.m. and 6 a.m., and on all Sundays and public
hol~days an additional charge of $2.50 shall be made for such service.
(2) The pilotage dues calcnlated in accordance with the provisions of paragraph (1) of this bye-law shall cover pilotage of any ship to or from the Grier Channel and Basin when the pilot boards it, or leaves it at anchor or under weigh, within the limits of Port-of-Spain harbour.
(3) "Whenever a ship is shifted in the Basin and the operation necessitates letting go and re-securing at another berth, half the pilotage dues calculated in accordance with sub-paragraphs (aJ and (b) of paragraph (1) of this bye-law shall be charged. •
. (4) Whenever a ship is shifted along the King's Wharf without letting go, a pIlot need not be employed. If a pilot is employed the pilotage dues shall be as set out in the next preceding paragraph of this bye-law. .
(5) In the case of pilotage at out ports, the pilot shall embark within five miles, but not less than one mile, of the berth. .
14. Pilotage dues in respect of services rendered by licensed pilots in non-compulsory areas shall be calculated in accordance with the following provisions of this bye-Iaw:- .

(b) from inside the Bocas to Carenage, Teteron, ChaguarCl-mas
Bay, Port-of-Spain, San Fernando, Bri:ghton or Point Fortin,

(3) The maximum charge for piloting a ship (outside compulsory pilotage areas) within the Gulf of Pari a shall in no case exceed the dues which would be payable in respect of a ship the net registered tonnage' of which is twelve thousansi tons.
15. (1) A pilot shall be entitled to detention money in accordance wit.q. the provisions of section 21 of the Ordinance at the rate of one dollar for each hour or part thereof and shall have the like remedy for recovery of the same as is provided in the Ordinance for recovery of pilotage dues.
(2) An attendance fee of $7.50 in lieu of detention shall be charged in respect of a pilot ordered for a vessel when such order is subsequently cancelled: Provided always that notice of cancellation shall be accepted if received prior to the departure of the pilot afloat when no charge shall be made.
16. If any boat or ship having on board a licensed pilot leads any ship which has not a licensed pilot on board, in a non-compulsory area, when the last mentioned ship cannot through stress of weather or other exceptional circumstances be boarded, the pilot so leading the last mentioned ship, at the request of the master thereof, shall be entitled to full pilotage rates as if he had actually been on board and had charge of that ship.
17. A pilot who is not a member of the Pilots' Association shall collect all fees due to him, and members of the Association shall make arrangements for the collection by the secretary of all fees due to them.
18. The Secretary-Accountant shall make an annual audited return to the Authority of all revenue and expenditure of the Association, and when required to do so by the Authority shall render supplementary returns.
The Secretary-Accountant shall place annually before the Authority an audited statement of the Provident Fund accounts of the Association, and a certificate that all insurances required by Clause 4 of the Articles of Association are in force.
The Secretary-Accountant shall furnish the Authority monthly with a statement giving the following particulars in respect of each pilot for the preceding month:-
(a) Number and description of pilotage movements performed daily.
(b) Time involved over each movement.
(e) Leave and/or sick periods, if. any, with dates.
(el) Stand off periods with dates.
19. Every pilot on being licensed, and every pilot already licensed, shall execute a bond for five hundred dollars in such form as the Authority may direct with a view to the limitation of his liability for neglect or want of skill to that amount as prescribed by section 24 of the Ordinance.
20. A master or mate of a ship applying for a pilotage certificate shall not be examined unless he holds a certificate as master or mate issued by the Government of Trinidad and Tobago, or an equivalent or higher certificate, and has made six voyages to the compulsory pilotage area for which he applies for a licence.
The application may be made as soon as six voyages have been completed, provided they have been made during the preceding twelve months. No application shall be entertained unless supported by a certificate of good character given by, the employers of the applicant; provided that in the case of droghers, masters and mates holding c~rtificates as masters or mates issued by the Government of Trinidad and Tobago will only be required to make six voyages assisted by a licensed pilot within a period of three months to each particular compulsory pilotage area before application for a certificate is made in respect of such area. Such aplication must also be supported by a certificate of good character as aforesaid.
21. A pilotage certificate shall not be renewed without re-examination unless the certificated officer has made not less than. three voyages to the compulsory pilotage area referred to in the certificate during the twelve months immediately preceding his application for the r"newal of. his certificate.
22. A master or mate shall not receive a pilotage certificate for any piiotage area until he has passed an examination before the committee appointed under bye-law 26, and unless he satisfies the requirements of sub-paragraphs (a), (g) and (h) of bye-law 3 (1).' .
23. The fees to be paid for the issue and renewal of pilotage certificates shall be in accordance withSchedllle II to these Bye-laws: Provided that,a pilotage certificate shall not be issued or renewed until the applicant has produced a medical certificate from a practitioner approved by the Authority to the effect that the applicant's eyesight is normal as regards form and colour visions.
24. The holder of a pilotage certificate shall produce on demand to any harbour master, pilot, officer of Police or Customs, his pilotage certificate when piloting within an area specified in the Second Schedule to the Ordinance.
Any such person failing to comply with the bye-law shall be liable to a fine of ten dollars.
25. Every holder of a pilotage certificate when in charge of a vessel in anv compulsory area specified in the Second Schedule to the Ordinance shall cause by day the international code flag" H" to be flown .from the jumper stay, and by night a white light over a red light in a vertical line not less th::m six feet apart to be carried in a similar position. Any such person failing to comply with this bye-law shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine of ten dollars.
26. A committee of the Authority appointed to conduct examinations under these Bye-laws shall consist of the Harbour Master, who shall be Chairman, and two members of the Authority.
27. In addition to ships exempted from compulsory pilotage under section 16 (3) of the Ordinance, all British ships under 600 gross tons trading locally within the meaning of section 16 (4) of the Ordinance and registered under the provisions of the Merchant Shipping Act, 1894, shall be exempted from compulsory pilotage if not carrying passengers .
28. (1) The Pilotage Authority shall meet on the third Monday in each calendar month to transact business: Provided that should that day be a public holiday, the meeting shall be postponed to the following Monday or such other day as the Chairman may appoint. .
Should for any reason business be not concluded at a regular meeting, the meeting may be adjourned to such date as the Chairman may decide.
(2) It shall be competent for the Chairman to call by notice in writing an emergency meeting of the Authority at any time, should he consider the business sufficiently urgent.
29. The Pilotage Authority shall consider at its regular meetings all business placed before it in connection with-
(a) recommendations to be made for the amendment or alteration of the
Ordinance or Bye-laws dealing with pilotage;
(b) complaints against pilots or holders of pilotage certificates;
(c) proposals to vary fees for pilotage licences or certificates;
(d) the disposal of funds accruing to the Authority;
(e) proposals to engage apprentices to the pilotage -service;
(f) suggestions for the improvement of the pilotage service;
(g) any business arising under these Bye-laws which. is properly before the
Authority. .
30. All fees payable under Schedule II of these Bye-laws shall be credited to the Authority and shall be used by them to defray all just expenses lawfully. incurred by the Authority provided that any surplus shall be applied as provided under section 6 of the Ordinance ..
31. The following fees shall be paid by all vessels on every occasion when under pilotage-
$
(i) within the declared limits of Port-of-Spain harbour 5.00
(ii) within the area from the Bocas to the western declared limit'of
Port-of-Spain harbour and bounded on the south by the parallel of 100 35' North 15.00
Provided that the payment of fees prescribed at (i) and (ii) shall be opt~o~al in the case of vessels proceeding under pilotage froin within the declared hmlts of Port-of-Spain harbour to any out port or vice versa, and that the fee prescribed at (i) shall not apply in the case of vessels when shifting berth at the King's Wharf.
32. Any pilot who contravenes or fails to comply with any of these Bye-laws, or any order or direction given or requirement imposed under any of these Bye-laws, shall be guilty of a breach of that bye-law, and shall for each such breach, subject to any special provisions contained in these Bye-laws, be liable on summary con-viction to a fine of forty-eight dollars (without prejudice to the powers of the Authority under the Ordinance to revoke or suspend his licence).
